 ## Accepted risks
 - Every accepted risk (`CR4-F-01` second half, `CR3-F-11`, `S17-sibling-arms`, `S16-decompress`, `claim-425`, `CR5-F-01`) is verbatim in [handoff-archive.md](handoff-archive.md) §Trimmed 2026-10-17 (`097-yield`); each keeps its own `Reported:` state. No new accepted risk was added by run 49 or run 50 — `S21` CE-2 was `absorbed` into `T021.003`, not accepted.
 
 ## Elevated to user
 
 - `S15-CE-1` — a proxied answer can arrive labelled with an encoding the proxy already undid, and a
   conforming client then fails on bytes that are plain (`curl: (61) Error while processing content
   unencoding: incorrect header check` — reproduced end-to-end through the real routes against a
   compressing peer, `evidence/s15-reach-and-base-comparison.txt` §1e). Trigger is narrow: it needs an
   upstream or gateway in front of it that *accepts* compression, and the deployed vLLM/uvicorn does not,
   at any size, on any offer. Costs one broken proxied request per affected call when it does fire; the
   upstream having succeeded is what makes it look like the proxy's fault. Proper fix lives in
   `buster_ripper/utils.py`'s shared filter or in the proxy's egress `Accept-Encoding` posture — a product
   decision, not a defect fix: **relay decoded and stop labelling**, **ask for `identity` and relay
   verbatim**, or **relay the wire bytes and decode only for the proxy's own parsing** (the last collides
   with `messages.py:272/276` and `chat.py:163`, which need the parsed form). Roadmap handle:
   `roadmap.md` §RM-008. `Reported: —`
 - `CR3-F-11` — a `message_start` SSE event split across network chunks is silently missed, and the turn's
   session stats (TTFT + input tokens) never record; what happens: one turn's compaction inputs are missing and
   the session-trigger nudge rides the previous turn's numbers; how narrow the trigger: the chunk boundary must
   land inside the first SSE event, on a self-healing path (the next turn re-records). Cost when it fires: one
   turn's telemetry, no client-visible harm. Proper fix lives in `buster_ripper/utils.py:91`'s per-chunk scan
   (or a relay-side carry buffer) — a small change on the accepted relay surface, deliberately not made mid-wave:
   the behavior is **base parity** (`buster_ripper_legacy.py:674-676`, re-verified), so nothing in this batch
   regressed it. Roadmap handle: `roadmap.md` §RM-009. `Reported: —`
 - `S17-sibling-arms` — an upstream **timeout** on three of the four proxy arms is answered by aiohttp, not by this
   proxy: the deadline the shared session arms (`total=600` inherited by `messages.py:265-297`; 300 s at
   `chat.py:139/150`; 60 s at `passthrough.py:27/48`) raises bare `asyncio.TimeoutError`, which those arms'
   `except aiohttp.ClientError` does not name (rows 330/331), so the protocol's `:518` arm answers with a generated
   **504** + `text/plain` + a traceback log instead of the site's own 502 + `{"error": "Upstream request failed"}` +
   `"Upstream error: %s"`. What happens and how narrow it is: any upstream that takes longer than the armed budget
   on a non-streaming call — ordinary network time at 60 s, less ordinary at 600 s, but reachable at all three; the
   `/v1/messages` **streaming** path is not in this set (closed by `T018` step 2). Cost when it fires: a client that
   parses the proxy's error envelope gets a body it did not expect, and the operator's log says "Error handling
   request" with a traceback rather than naming the upstream. Proper fix lives in the three arms' class lists (or in
   one shared wrapper) — small in lines, not in cost: `chat.py` sits at **300/300** raw lines, so it drags §RM-001's
   route restructure with it, and it changes a client-visible status (504 → 502) that **base settled worse** (base
   had no arm at all: an `httpx` timeout propagated to Starlette and became a 500 — `buster_ripper_legacy.py`, no
   `httpx.HTTPError` anywhere). Needs no decision to *route*: it is out of this session's scope and blocks no
   done-when. Roadmap handle: `roadmap.md` §RM-010. `Reported: —`
 - `S16-decompress` — the aiohttp server decompresses **request** bodies transparently, and nothing in the relay says
   so. What happens: a client sending `Content-Encoding: gzip` has its body decoded before the handler sees it, so the
   body forwarded upstream is plain bytes travelling under the peer's own `Content-Encoding` label
   (`passthrough.py:14`/`:31`; the two `json=` paths re-serialize a body the peer labelled encoded), and a body whose
   *decompressed* output exceeds `2**25` in one feed raises `DecompressSizeError` — not an `HTTPException` — so the
   protocol's generic arm answers a generated **500** where a size limit would want a 413. How narrow the trigger: it
   needs a compressing client, which nothing in this package's documented surface asks for and the deployed launcher
   never sends; the 32 MiB half needs a body of that size after inflation. Cost when it fires: one failed or
   mislabelled upstream request, no data loss, nothing silent on the common path. Proper fix lives in the request
   direction's content-coding posture — the shared filter (`utils.py:20-26`), an egress-side re-encode, or a
   deliberate `Content-Encoding` drop — which is the same **design** decision §RM-008 already holds for the *response*
   direction; base could not produce this state because it never decompressed a request body (zero
   `gzip`/`decompress`/`Content-Encoding` references in `buster_ripper_legacy.py`). Needs no decision to route; it
   blocks nothing in this wave. Roadmap handle: `roadmap.md` §RM-011. `Reported: —`

 ## Options (settled)
 
 Both dials settled by the user: trigger = "both triggers + shutdown" (`FK-001`); payload = the **collapsed format** (`FK-002`), so Option 3 is the pick. `US-007` added a third position to the payload dial: the record **encoding** is a configuration choice. `FK-007` asked only the human-facing axis, encoding set as fixed context — **(1)** the self-contained full-text encoding *is* the addition, no new tool; **(2)** a terminal viewer; **(3)** a one-shot "print turn N" command, `Recommendation: none`. **RESOLVED — option 1**: the offered set is the two collapsed encodings and **no tool**. ✅ **019 settles the last two open clauses**: `FK-002`'s pick is realized as **S-1** — the collapsed formats are **added beside** the documented layout, never in place of it — and **the default is `documented`**, not change-focused. The three values are one closed set (`F-24`); the documented layout is itself one value of the selector.
 
 | Option | Flush trigger | Flush payload | Write reduction | Status |
 |---|---|---|---|---|
 | 1 — both triggers + documented layout | time **and** volume **and** shutdown | documented per-turn files | 2N → N + F | **payload rejected** (`FK-002`) |
 | 2 — shutdown only + documented layout | shutdown only | documented per-turn files | 2N → N + 1 | **rejected** (`FK-001`) |
 | 3 — both triggers + collapsed single file | time **and** volume **and** shutdown | one collapsed per-session artifact, appended once per flush | 2N → F **when selected** | **SELECTED** (`FK-002`); addition [`FK-007`](decisions.md#fk-007) **option 1**. ⚠ **019**: the collapsed formats are **opt-in**; the default configuration produces the documented layout at `2N` |

 ## Goals and Constraints
 
 - Buffer captured messages in memory during message-interception/dumping mode (`--dump-dir` on `/v1/messages`) and write them to disk in
   bulk, reducing the number of write operations. Flush the buffered dump on Ctrl-C (SIGINT) and SIGTERM before the process terminates.
 - Non-goal / explicit acceptance: durability of buffered in-flight messages — a hard shutdown (e.g. SIGKILL) may kill them. ~~Preserve the documented dump output contract (`turn_000.json`, `turn_NNN.diff`, `index.txt`)~~ — **this non-goal is REVOKED by [`US-012`](decisions.md#us-012)** (round 14): preserving the documented layout is now a requirement, not an accepted loss. The proxy's behavior on `/v1/messages` is still preserved (`G-003`).
 - ⚠ **Caveat (019) — the reduction is OPT-IN, and the plan must not read as if the Topic were delivered by default.** The Topic says
   "buffer … and flush in bulk"; under S-1 that machinery is **inactive unless a collapsed format is selected**. A default run buffers
   nothing, starts no timer, and performs no shutdown flush. This is the direct entailment of [`US-012`](decisions.md#us-012)'s gating clause,
   disclosed here rather than decided silently: the switch is deferred to a `roadmap.md` entry, owned by a later session, triggered by the
   user's judgment of the `MS-002` comparison evidence *(corrected by 022)*. ⛔ **Fence — the tempting wrong reading**: *"the documented layout is retained"* does
   **not** mean *"a collapsed format may be the default."* Retention is about the writer surviving as a selectable branch; the default is a
   separate clause (`PI:T002/dump_format/default_format_is_documented`) and it is `documented`.
 - Constraint (write reduction): under the collapsed payload a flush costs **one** write regardless of how many turns it carries. For N turns
   flushed `F` times, writes are `F` versus `2N`, so the reduction is `1 − F/(2N)` — **floor 50%** at `F = N`, zero unreachable (an empty
   flush writes nothing, so `F ≤ N`). `F` is a per-`(sid, format)` count. **With no format configured the count is `2N`, not `F`** — the
   clause above is conditional on a collapsed format being selected. The residual way to lose `G-001` is a **rewrite-per-flush**
   artifact: count `F`, bytes `O(N²)` (witness B).
 - Constraint (coverage): `pyproject.toml:19` gates the suite at 80% and `dump.py` is **not** omitted. `.coverage` totals 506 stmts / 98 miss / 81%; with `K` new statements of which `U` uncovered, the gate holds iff **`U ≤ 3.2 + 0.2·K`**. The rewrite is **coverage-neutral to favourable**: `dump.py` carries 54 of the suite's 98 missed statements at 24%, and the three replaced write sites (`dump.py:82`, `:95`, `:104-106`) are inside that missed set. Binding fact is placement — `server.py` is coverage-omitted, so the timer **loop body** and the record builder must live in `dump.py` and be tested.

 **TC-102 / TC-103 / TC-104 — the collapsed artifact's shape, readability, and turn 0 (MS-002).**
 *Trigger*: a fresh session dir, `--dump-format <collapsed value>`, turns 0..2 sent, a flush triggered by the volume threshold.
 *Expected owner*: the format dispatch in `dump.py` selects the collapsed writer; `flush_all` appends once.
 *Visible result*: exactly one `<format>.log` for the configured value; no `turn_000.json`, `turn_NNN.diff`, `index.txt`; three records in arrival order; the filename states the configured value; turn 0's raw normalized JSON body **and** its rendering are recoverable; a reader can locate turn 2's change from the directory alone (TC-103: for `self-contained`, by comparing turn 2's record against turn 1's).
 *Forbidden result*: two artifacts for one configured format; a documented file appearing on the collapsed path; records out of arrival order; turn 0 stored only as a diff; a summary line that lost the turn number or the change counts.
 *Lifecycle*: append-only — the artifact is never rewritten; a second flush extends it.
 *Telemetry*: none.
 *Evidence source*: directory listing + artifact contents, per format.
 
 **TC-105 / TC-109 — the write-count and file-count measures (MS-002).**
 *Trigger*: two runs at the same N — one per collapsed format, one documented — with both flush parameters injected so `F` is chosen by the test. The documented half is `TC-108`'s run at `MS-001`; **the pair is completed here**.
 *Expected owner*: one write path per turn (`PI:T002/dump_format/one_write_path_per_turn`); `flush_all` writes once per flush.
 *Visible result*: instrumented write-bearing file operations `= F` collapsed vs `2N` documented; one write per flush of K ≥ 2 turns; **and the bytes written across the session track the pending batches, not the accumulated artifact**; files `1` collapsed vs `N + 1` documented. **The paired runs are the measurement `G-008`(b) requires; the `MS-002` Yield Phase records them side by side in its comparison record.**
 *Forbidden result*: `2N + F` in any configuration (witness C); a flush that writes once per pending turn (witness A); a rewrite-per-flush whose bytes grow `O(N²)` (witness B); a collapsed run that also writes the documented files.
 *Lifecycle*: counts are per session.
 *Telemetry*: none.
 *Evidence source*: the instrumented write counter (**call count and payload bytes**) + two directory listings, recorded as a pair + the `MS-002` Yield Phase's comparison record (a durable, human-readable artifact).
 
 **TC-106 — the volume trigger (MS-002).**
 *Trigger*: interval injected large so the timer cannot fire; turns appended until `pending_bytes` reaches `--dump-flush-bytes`.
 *Expected owner*: the append-time threshold check inside `dump_turn`, reading the same field the append wrote.
 *Visible result*: the pending turns are in the artifact with no shutdown and no timer tick.
 *Forbidden result*: turns accumulating past the threshold with nothing on disk until shutdown; a check evaluated before the append or off by one.
 *Lifecycle*: the check is collapsed-path only — on the documented path nothing accumulates and the check never fires.
 *Telemetry*: none.
 *Evidence source*: artifact contents before/after the threshold turn.
 
 **TC-108 — the documented format is preserved (MS-001).**
 *Trigger*: `--dump-format documented` (or none configured) at the same N as `TC-105`; no flush parameter is passed — the documented path writes per turn, so `MS-001` can run it.
 *Expected owner*: the format dispatch's documented branch — today's three write sites, retained.
 *Visible result*: `turn_000.json`, `turn_{N:03d}.diff`, `index.txt` with today's content and line format, at `2N` write-bearing operations. This is the documented half of the `G-008`(b) pair; `TC-105` supplies the collapsed half at `MS-002`.
 *Forbidden result*: a drifted `index.txt` line format; a missing or renamed documented file; a `.log` artifact on the documented path.
 *Lifecycle*: unchanged from today.
 *Telemetry*: none.
 *Evidence source*: listing + contents + the instrumented write count.
 
 **TC-107 — the default is the documented layout (MS-001).**
 *Trigger*: no `--dump-format` passed, no config override.
 *Expected owner*: `config.DUMP_FORMAT`'s `"documented"` default.

 *Lifecycle*: unchanged from today.
 *Telemetry*: none.

 **TC-110 / TC-111 / TC-112 — the flush triggers and timer resilience (MS-002).**
 *Trigger*: TC-110 — real SIGINT and (separately) SIGTERM to the proxy subprocess with turns pending; TC-111 — a small interval and an unreachable volume threshold; TC-112 — an injected raise inside one flush iteration.
 *Expected owner*: `on_cleanup` cancels the flush task, then flushes, then closes the shared session — in that order; the timer loop catches per-iteration exceptions and continues.
 *Visible result*: TC-110 — buffered turns on disk and a clean exit (exit 0, no traceback); TC-111 — the turn reaches the artifact while the process runs; TC-112 — the raise is logged and a later tick flushes successfully.
 *Forbidden result*: a traceback replacing `GracefulExit`; buffered turns lost at shutdown; a timer task that dies silently on its first exception; a flush that raises out of `on_cleanup`.
 *Lifecycle*: quiescence is established by waiting for process exit before inspecting the directory; TC-111 polls the artifact rather than sleeping a fixed multiple of the interval.
 *Telemetry*: none.
 *Evidence source*: subprocess + real signal + post-exit listing + exit code + stderr log record.
 
 **TC-003 / TC-113 / TC-114 — the upstream-TLS postures (MS-004).**

 *Expected owner*: the explicit `aiohttp.TCPConnector(ssl=not config.SKIP_TLS_VERIFY)` built inside `on_startup` and passed as `connector=` to the package's only `ClientSession` (`server.py:47`).
 *Visible result*: TC-003 — the request does not complete and the failure is a certificate-verification error; TC-113 — the same peer, same request, completes; TC-114 — every outbound call carries the one posture.
 *Forbidden result*: **W1** — a self-signed peer accepted with no opt-out (the connector built `ssl=False` unconditionally, or dropped); **W2** — the flag accepted and its global set but the connector built without it, so both postures behave identically; a per-call-site posture.

 *Evidence source*: the failed/completed request, its error class, and the connector construction.

 ## MVP floor
 
 The floor is what this session must deliver to be worth shipping at all:
 
 1. The dump payload becomes a single configured **format** with a closed set of
    three values (`documented`, `change-focused`, `self-contained`), default
    `documented` (`US-012`).
 2. The collapsed formats deliver **one bulk flush = one write** (`FK-002`'s
    surviving clause), so a session of N turns flushed F times costs `F`
    write-bearing operations instead of `2N` — reachable, witnessed, and
    documented.
 3. The documented per-turn layout is **unaffected**: with no format configured,
    `turn_000.json`, `turn_{N:03d}.diff` and `index.txt` are produced exactly as
    today, at today's `2N` writes (`G-008`(a)).
 4. The flush-trigger policy (`FK-001`) is implemented for the collapsed path:
    volume threshold, interval, and shutdown flush.
 5. The upstream-TLS opt-out cluster (`FK-006`/`FK-007`/`FK-008`, `T006`) ships as
    decided in 017.
 6. The comparison evidence `G-008`(b) requires exists at `MS-002`, on named
    measures **M1** (write-bearing operations) and **M2** (files created).
    *(Corrected by `022`: the collapsed half of the pair needs a flush, and the
    flush triggers land with `T003` — `MS-001` cannot produce it.)*
 
 Everything below is charted **above** that floor. Nothing below is required for the
 session to be complete or correct.
 
 ---
 
 ### Switch the default dump format — DEFERRED (above MVP floor)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-013`.
+
 Core delivered: the collapsed formats are implemented, selectable
 (`--dump-format change-focused | self-contained`), documented, and witnessed by
 `VP-T002-write-count-reduced` and `VP-T002-file-count-reduced`; the documented
 format remains the default and is byte-identical to today, witnessed by
 `VP-T002-documented-format-preserved`.
 
 Deferred: performing the switch itself — flipping the default to a collapsed format
 and/or deleting the documented writer's three write sites (`dump.py:82`, `:95`,
 `:105`) once the collapsed format has been demonstrated better and the user has
 judged it worth switching to.
 
 Safe because: the switch is a one-value change in `config.py` plus a branch
 deletion — no data migration, no contract break while both formats are shipped and
 documented, and no reader depends on the new default until it lands. The decision to
 make it is gated on evidence that this session produces rather than assumes: the
 paired runs at `MS-002` on measures M1 and M2.
 
 - **Owner**: a later session.
 - **Trigger**: the user's judgment of the `MS-002` comparison evidence — a
   demonstration the new format is better, and a decision it is worth switching to
   (`US-012`, `decisions.md#us-012`). Not a date, not a metric threshold alone. The
   evidence is the `MS-002` Yield Phase's comparison record (a durable,
   human-readable artifact), not an in-test assertion.
 - **Source**: `US-012` (2026-09-16) — *"Old behavior should stay unaffected until
   we prove a new one is better and it's worth switching to."*
 - **What the switch would have to decide, not assume**: whether the demonstration
   rests on the write-count proxy (M1) or on physical flash wear (M3), which is
   **not measurable in this repository** — the byte figures carried through this plan
   come from `007` and were not re-derived (`/Volumes/data` is unmounted).
 
 ### Dump machine record — DEFERRED (above MVP floor)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-014`.
+
 Core delivered: nothing of it. The dump payload carries human-readable records only;
 the closed format set (`documented`, `change-focused`, `self-contained`) has no
 machine-oriented value.
 
 Deferred: a machine-oriented dump format — a compact record aimed at tooling rather
 than at a person reading the file — as a fourth value of the format set.
 
 Safe because: the format set is closed and validated (`--dump-format` rejects an
 unknown value), so adding a fourth value is an additive change to one tuple, one
 record builder, and the README's format table; nothing existing is displaced, and no
 shipped value changes meaning. It was measured at ≈1.047× the logical bytes of the
 change-focused record and was not taken because the reduction it might have supported
 is on the physical axis, which this repository cannot measure.
 
 - **Owner**: a later session.
 - **Trigger**: a consumer that reads the dump programmatically, or a measurement of the
   physical axis.
 - **Source**: `FK-007`'s `Impact.Roadmap` (element (a) — the pick did not take it).
 
 ### Human-facing dump tool — DEFERRED (above MVP floor)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-015`.
+
 Core delivered: nothing of it. The dump is read by opening the files and reading the
 README's *"Reading the index"* section; no tool is shipped.
 
 Deferred: a human-facing viewer/tool that renders a session's dump — reconstructing
 the per-turn view from a collapsed artifact, or browsing a session's turn history
 without reading raw files.
 
 Safe because: the collapsed formats keep every turn's content in the artifact
 (`change-focused` carries the unified diff per turn; `self-contained` carries every
 turn's full rendering), so the data a viewer needs is present and losslessly ordered
 in the file. A viewer is an addition over a complete artifact, not a repair of an
 incomplete one.
 
 - **Owner**: a later session.
 - **Trigger**: a user asking to read a collapsed dump without opening it by hand.
 - **Source**: `FK-007`'s `Impact.Roadmap` (element (b) — the pick shipped the
   self-contained encoding instead), and `US-008` — *"add something more
   human-visible or human-friendly."*
 
 ### Chat route's post-commit second head — OUT OF SCOPE (066-01-yield P2, routed by `codify-scope` rules 1–2)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-016`.
+
 Found: while verifying `T013`'s header-parity wave, [066-01-yield](iterations/066-01-yield.md) P2 traced the same
 defect class that member G-1 closed in `/v1/messages` still live at its sibling boundary.
 `buster_ripper/routes/chat.py:174-188` (`_relay_chat_stream`; was `:170-184` when this section was written — see
 the 2026-10-06 amendment) calls `await response.prepare(request)` at `:184` (was `:180`) and
 then relays inside `async with`, so a mid-relay `ClientPayloadError` escapes to `_make_chat_request`'s
 `except aiohttp.ClientError` at `:155-157` (was `:151-153`), which returns a **fresh** `web.json_response({"error": …}, status=502)`.
 `prepare()`'s re-entry guard is per-response-object while the writer is per-request, so that object is prepared onto
 the already-committed stream: a second status line inside the first response's body, and no terminal the client can
 trust. That is `DC-S11-CE1-r1` verbatim, at a route `057` §C never enumerated.
 
 Why it is not a `T013` item: no `T013` clause names it — F-4's scope is the five filter sites, G-1's is the messages
 relay per `057` §C and `DC-G-1-*`. It is byte-unchanged across the whole wave (`verified: git diff 41d3fc6..HEAD --
 buster_ripper/routes/chat.py` touches only `_prepare_headers` and its helper), it gates no accepted criterion, and
 folding it into the wave would have shipped an unattributed behavior change inside a wave whose members are
 deliberately one commit each and separately attributable. It is not a `codify-scope` **rule 3** case either: this
 session's retry needed no trustworthy result from it — the P1 was entirely inside `_prepare_headers`'s selection.
 
 Reach and cost of leaving it: `latent` — a streaming `/v1/chat/completions` whose upstream body dies after the head
 (an eval harness against a worker that goes away mid-generation). Consequence is a client parsing a status out of a
 200 body, the exact harm `T013` was raised to remove elsewhere in the same package.
 
 What the fix owes, so it is not re-derived later: `R-E`'s shape a second time (one post-commit arm that logs
 position-not-cause and returns the committed object, with `CancelledError` left propagating), its own witness set at
 the raw-bytes tier for the chat route, and a `seam-S11` reading — the seam currently records the messages relay's
 semantics only. Do it on its own branch from main.
 
 Handle: `roadmap.md` §Chat route's post-commit second head. Pointer kept (not resolved) at
 `QB:routes-chat/post-commit-second-head` in `quality-backlog.md`.
 
 **Amended 2026-10-06 by the CR2 closure-packet routing (ledger `DC-CR2-F-4-r1`):** the three line pointers above
 were refreshed to the bytes at `dbd3072` (drift caused by `T013` retry-1's `_prepare_headers` rewrite; the relay
 itself remains byte-unchanged and the defect still holds — re-verified). The post-071 `codex-review` batch brought
 a **byte-level repro** — two `HTTP/1.1` status lines in one response, the synthetic 502 head inside the committed
 200's body — which *confirms* the harm this section already states ("a second status line inside the first
 response's body, and no terminal the client can trust"); the packet's premise that the record here understated it
 as a "partial body without a terminator" matched nothing in the session and was corrected on the way in. The
 reviewer's elevation note is recorded for the owner of this handle: **stronger confirmed severity is the reason
 this slice should not sit indefinitely behind other roadmap rows.** The `codify-scope` rules 1–2 ownership ruling
 above was explicitly rechecked against the repro and stands **unchanged** — no session gate needs this fix, and
 the debts listed under "What the fix owes" are unchanged.

 ### RM-001: Route-handler LNCP restructure — DEFERRED (design decision under live bindings)

 ### RM-008: Response-relay content-coding posture — DEFERRED (1% category, `seam-S15` CE-1; routed 2026-10-06)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-008`.
+
 Deferred: the shared filter `buster_ripper/utils.py:29-56` (`response_headers`; the locator moved from the
 `:27-40` this row was written against, because `T017` added the posture paragraph at `:46-50` that points here)
 relays the
 upstream's `Content-Encoding` over body bytes the proxy's own client already decompressed (`ClientSession`
 default `auto_decompress=True`; the package names the parameter nowhere —
 `grep -rn "auto_decompress" buster_ripper/` → zero), so a compressing upstream
 yields a labelled-encoding answer whose body is plain and whose `Content-Length` matches the plain bytes.
 `codify-agents` → Reach Gate puts this at **latent**: the proxy solicits the encoding on both egress arms and
 has no arm that declines decoding, and the harm is reproducible end-to-end through the real routes
 (`curl: (61) Error while processing content unencoding: incorrect header check`), but the deployed upstream
 (`http://hyperipper.local:30001`, vLLM/uvicorn — the endpoint the live `QAP-MS001`/`QAP-MS002` gates run against)
 answers identity to `identity`, `gzip, deflate` and `gzip, deflate, br, zstd` on every path probed, including a
 66 535-byte `text/plain` `/metrics`, and no test, gate, doc or evidence artifact on this tree has ever named a
 `Content-Encoding` on this path. Full transcript: `evidence/s15-reach-and-base-comparison.txt` §1.
 
 **Corrected 2026-10-17 (`decisions.md` §US-009):** the endpoint named in this row's basis is
 `http://hyperipper.local:30000`, not `:30001` — `:30001` is a peer Buster Ripper instance, not the model
 server. The encoding observation was made through that peer proxy, whose shared filter relays the upstream's
 `Content-Encoding` unchanged (`buster_ripper/utils.py:29-56`), so it still describes the model server's behaviour
 as relayed; whether the `latent` ruling's basis survives is this row owner's re-read, not re-decided here. The
 `QAP-MS001`/`QAP-MS002` gates are owed a re-run against `:30000`.
 
 Why this is a ruling and not a repair: the behavior **is** base parity. `buster_ripper_legacy.py:630-637` kept
 the name (its drop-set was the eight hop-by-hop names only — it did not even drop `content-length`) and httpx
 decoded `.content` the same way while advertising `Accept-Encoding` from its own defaults, so the monolith
 carried the identical mismatch at the same three boundaries. This session's settled direction at those
 boundaries is `T013`'s — *restore base header handling* — so any remedy is a deliberate **parity deviation**, and
 the three shapes are not interchangeable: drop `content-encoding` at the filter (decoded bytes, no label) /
 force `Accept-Encoding: identity` on egress (upstream bytes arrive verbatim, and `T015`'s accepted required
 behavior 2 becomes actually true) / relay the wire bytes with `auto_decompress=False` (verbatim *and*
 honest, but breaks the more common case — `messages.py:272/276` and `chat.py:163` parse the body for token
 stats and the overflow check). `seam-S15`'s Note N3 records that nothing in the six member claims is normative
 about the posture, and no session authority ranks them. Per `codify-scope` this is the **1% category**: recorded
 once, no repair attempted here, and it **gates nothing** — no task, no Fork, no wave; `FK-011` remains the
 only open Fork. The claim rows behind it (`claims.md` 384, 385, 386, 387, 389) are routed
 `accepted-consequence` and their `HOLD` states stand as this row's library evidence.
 
 **Do not confuse with what was fixed here:** the sibling finding on the same composition, **CE-2**
 (repeated header names folded to the last occurrence), is **not** base parity — the base's client merged
 occurrences, so it delivered every value — and it was repaired in-session as `T017`, which **landed and was
 accepted-local on 2026-10-06**; the owed `seam-S15` recomposition (basis `sha256:d790d12b…`) shows the fold gone,
 so CE-2's chain is closed and `S15` is terminal with `Routing: accepted-consequence`. A future reader of
 `QB:utils/response-header-relay-fidelity` must not carry that line's original "both are base parity" framing
 forward: it is correct for CE-1 and refuted for CE-2.
 
 **And do not read this row as owning occurrence shape.** `T017`'s repair means a duplicated
 *single-valued* name (`Date`, `Server`) now reaches the client on several field-lines where it used to reach it
 on one — `seam-S15`'s Note **N5**, adjudicated by the recomposition pass and held **accepted** with reach
 `reached`: no admissible remediation differs from the current relay, because collapsing or merging a duplicate
 discards a value the base delivered in the case the deployment actually produces. The three postures below are
 about **which names** travel and whether a label matches its bytes; they neither include nor exclude a
 name-arity table, and adding one would be a new ruling, not a clause of this row. Full record:
 `handoff-context.md` §Accepted risks (`S15-N5`) and
 [076-assumption-decide](iterations/076-assumption-decide.md).
 
 - **Owner**: the header-lane design pass (a product-posture ruling on relayed names, not a remediation wave).
 - **Trigger**: the next decision about which response header *names* the proxy relays, **or** any upstream
   change that makes compression reachable — a gateway in front of `--upstream`, a backend whose server enables
   response compression, a client report of a decode failure through the proxy, or installing `brotli` /
   `brotli-cffi` / `backports.zstd`, which additionally fires `seam-S15`'s Note N1 and requires recomposition.
 - **Source**: `assumptions/compositions/seam-S15.md` §Counterexample CE-1 + §Notes N1/N3;
   `evidence/s15-reach-and-base-comparison.txt` §1; routing record `iterations/074-assumption-decide.md`
   (§S15-CE-1) and `handoff-context.md` §Accepted risks / §Elevated to user; `QB:utils/response-header-relay-fidelity`.
+- **Extended by `T024` (2026-10-18)**: the same helper's return container is a mutable `CIMultiDict`
+  (`utils.py:52`), one mutating caller away from silently collapsing occurrences (`CIMultiDict.__setitem__`
+  is `popall` + `add`); returning `CIMultiDictProxy(result)` is the decision this row's owner now also
+  carries — `QB:utils/response-headers-mutable-return`.
 
 ### RM-009: `find_message_start` per-chunk scan misses a split `message_start` — DEFERRED (1% category, `codify-scope`; routed 2026-10-06 by the CR3 routing)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-009`.
+
 Deferred: `buster_ripper/utils.py:91`'s `find_message_start` decodes and line-scans **each network chunk in
 isolation**, so a `message_start` event whose bytes straddle a chunk boundary is never recognized — that turn's
 TTFT + input tokens go unrecorded (`_note_message_start` at `routes/messages.py:200-215` re-scans every chunk
 until it succeeds, so the next turn self-heals). Reported by `CR3-F-11` (P3) in the CR3 batch
 ([079](../iterations/079-codex-review.md) §CR3-F-11; disposition and reach in
 [080](../iterations/080-assumption-decide.md): reach **latent**, 1%-category).
 
 Why this is a deferral and not a wave member: the behavior is **base parity** — the pre-refactor monolith scans
 the same way per chunk (`buster_ripper_legacy.py:674-676`, re-verified by the routing pass) — so the branch
 carried no regression into review; the harm is one turn's telemetry on a rare timing, it blocks no session gate,
 and the fix (carry the undelimited tail across chunks, or scan the accumulated stream head) lands on the
 accepted relay surface, which the CR3 wave's must-not-touch sets deliberately keep cold.
 
 What the fix owes, so it is not re-derived later: a chunk-carry (or accumulate-until-first-event) form of
 `find_message_start`'s contract with a fail-first witness feeding `message_start` bytes split at every position
 of one event (the split point the review needs is inside the `data:` line, not merely between events), while
 keeping the current "found once, then stop scanning" behavior and the relay's byte-for-byte chunk passthrough
 untouched.
 
 - **Owner**: the streaming-parse quality pass (with `roadmap.md` §RM-001's handler restructures as a likely
   shared visit).
 - **Trigger**: a field report of missing/zeroed per-session stats or a never-firing session-trigger nudge on a
   streaming client, **or** the next restructure touching `find_message_start`/`_note_message_start`.
 
 ### RM-010: the still-armed transport arms do not own the timeout class, and the generated-error envelope is unwritten — OUT OF SCOPE (routed 2026-10-06 by the run-42 composition pass)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-010`.
+
 Deferred: three of the four upstream call sites still arm a `ClientTimeout.total` and catch only
 `aiohttp.ClientError` — `_non_stream_single_request` (`routes/messages.py:265-297`, inheriting `server.py:67`'s
 600 s), `_make_chat_request` (`routes/chat.py:139`/`:150`, 300 s) and `handle_passthrough`
 (`routes/passthrough.py:27`/`:48`, 60 s). Per the session's own third-party evidence the class a firing **total**
 raises is bare `asyncio.TimeoutError`, which is **not** a `ClientError` (`assumptions/claims.md` rows 330/331,
 both `HOLD`; `verdicts/claim-331.md` for the pass-through at `client.py:793-798`), so at those three sites a
 deadline expiry escapes the arm and is answered by aiohttp's protocol at `web_protocol.py:518` with a
 **generated 504** + `text/plain` + a traceback log — never the site's declared 502 +
 `{"error": "Upstream request failed"}` + `"Upstream error: %s"`. The paired missing contract, named by both
 `S16`-(b) and `S17-N3`: *the status and body this proxy owes a client for a failure it does not author* —
 aiohttp's generated `400`/`500`/`504` are English `text/plain` documents while every repo-authored client-facing
 failure is JSON `{"error": …}` (`assumptions/compositions/S16-body-limit.md` §What Is Required (b);
 `assumptions/compositions/S17-stream-deadline.md` §Notes N3, whose gap row **230** already fills).
 
 Why this is a deferral and not a wave member: **base settled it worse.** `buster_ripper_legacy.py` has no
 upstream-transport arm at all — no `httpx.HTTPError`, no `TransportError`, no `"Upstream error"` string anywhere in
 the file — so an `httpx` timeout propagated out of `proxy_messages` (`:639`), the chat handler (`:811`) and the
 passthrough (`:901`) and became a Starlette 500. The 502 is **branch-authored** (`T013`–`T017`, witnessed by
 `tests/test_upstream_answer_relay.py`), so the parity authority that ranks the CR3 wave obliges nothing here; the
 gap is between the branch's own arm and the class set aiohttp hands it. `DC-CR3-F-03-r1` said the same thing in
 the negative when it rejected "drop/raise the shared session's 600 s" for silently moving the non-stream arm, and
 carried the sibling observation "so Yield Red does not read silence as oversight" — this row is where that
 observation finally gets an owner. The `/v1/messages` **streaming** path is *not* in this set: `T018` step 2 arms
 no deadline there (base's posture, `buster_ripper_legacy.py:664`), which is what closes `S17-CE-1`/`CE-2`.
 
 What the fix owes, so it is not re-derived later: a posture decision first, then the mechanism. (1) **Which status
 is the proxy's answer to "the upstream exceeded its deadline"** — the site's existing 502 (class-list widening:
 `except (aiohttp.ClientError, asyncio.TimeoutError)`, which is the same shape at all three arms) or the protocol's
 504 (leave the arms, pin the observable, and give the generated body a name in the docs). Neither is base-parity
 ranked; both are defensible, which is precisely why it needs a design pass rather than a wave. (2) Whichever way
 it goes, the repair must state its scope as the **whole** arm family (four call sites including `compaction.py`'s
 poll and `backend.py`'s probe, which already answer differently — `:55`'s `except Exception` covers the class) so
 one label does not mean three things across the package, and each arm needs a fail-first witness whose pre-fix
 failure names `asyncio.TimeoutError` as the class that escaped — a witness that only asserts "a 5xx came back"
 proves nothing about which arm ran. (3) `S17-N4` rides the same decision: the armed budget is **per upstream
 attempt**, so the SGLang overflow retry (`messages.py:99-114`) can spend ≈3× the number for one client-visible
 request — say now whether that is intended, and if not, where the per-request ceiling lives. (4) Capacity:
 `routes/chat.py` is at **300/300** raw lines, so any added import or arm breaches the FL-LNCP ceiling — §RM-001's
 handler restructure is the natural shared visit, and this row must not be paid for by a new inline helper.
 
 - **Owner**: the transport-error-envelope design pass, jointly with §RM-008 (response-direction content coding) and
   §RM-001 (the handler restructures) — the three meet at the same relay surface.
 - **Trigger**: the first field report of a client unable to parse a proxy error body, **or** any new config channel
   for upstream timeouts (which turns `S17-N2`'s currently-inert `None`/`0`/omitted-`total` spellings into live
   inputs and re-composes `S17` per its `Invalidates On` 5), **or** the next restructure touching any of the four
   arms.
 - **Source**: `assumptions/compositions/S17-stream-deadline.md` (CE-1's class facts, `N3`, `N4`) and
   `S16-body-limit.md` ((b)'s envelope contract), routed by
   [082-assumption-decide](../iterations/082-assumption-decide.md); `handoff-context.md` §Accepted risks
   (`S17-sibling-arms`) and §Elevated to user (`Reported: —`).
 
 ### RM-011: the server decompresses request bodies and nothing downstream says so — OUT OF SCOPE (1% category, `codify-scope`; routed 2026-10-06 by the run-42 composition pass)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-011`.
+
 Deferred: aiohttp's request parser inflates a request body whose `Content-Encoding` names `gzip|deflate|br|zstd`
 (`http_parser.py:556-558, 799-804`, with `auto_decompress` on for the server too —
 `web_protocol.py:206, 224-232`), so every handler sees **decompressed** bytes. Three consequences, none of them a
 finding on the CR3 surfaces and all of them recorded so they do not vanish: (i) any future `client_max_size` bounds
 *decompressed* bytes rather than wire bytes — moot while `T018` step 1 sets `0`, live the moment a cap channel
 appears (`S16` dimension 1 is recomposed on that landing either way); (ii) `DeflateBuffer`'s
 `DEFAULT_MAX_DECOMPRESS_SIZE = 2**25` (`http_parser.py:972, 1040-1044`, `compression_utils.py:37`) raises
 `DecompressSizeError` — `PayloadEncodingError ← BadHttpMessage ← HttpProcessingError`, **not** an
 `HTTPException` — so it lands in `web_protocol.py:522`'s generic arm and the client is answered a generated **500**
 where a size rejection wants a 413 (row 230 for the dispatch); and (iii) the relay never reconciles the label with
 the bytes: `passthrough.py:14` reads inflated bytes and `:31` forwards them under the peer's own
 `Content-Encoding` (`utils.py:20-26` keeps the name), and the `json=` paths at `messages.py:139`/`chat.py:141`
 re-serialize a body the peer labelled encoded, so the upstream is handed plain bytes with a compressed promise.
 
 Why this is a deferral and not a wave member: real but rare, with no obvious fix where it shows — it needs a
 client that compresses a request body, which nothing in this package's documented surface asks for and the
 deployed launcher never sends, and the 32 MiB half needs that body to exceed `2**25` *after* inflation. The cause
 lives in the request direction's content-coding posture, which is a **design** posture, not a defect elimination;
 and base could not produce this state at all, because base read bodies through Starlette and never decompressed a
 request body (zero `gzip`/`decompress`/`Content-Encoding` references in `buster_ripper_legacy.py`) — so nothing the
 CR3 wave is repairing regressed it. The agent did no repair and built no proof.
 
 What the fix owes, so it is not re-derived later: the same three-way posture §RM-008 names for the response
 direction, decided for requests instead — **drop `Content-Encoding` on egress when the body was inflated**, **ask
 peers not to encode request bodies and reject `Content-Encoding` on ingress with a client-facing status the proxy
 authors**, or **re-encode on egress** (the heaviest, and the only one that preserves the peer's framing for an
 upstream that wants it). Whichever is chosen owes: a witness per route class that sends a gzip-encoded
 `/v1/messages` body and asserts what the upstream actually receives (a stub that decodes and re-encodes is the
 only way to observe this — the current `tests/qa/` stubs do not compress request bodies), one witness for the
 `DecompressSizeError` shape asserting a status the proxy names rather than a generated 500, and an explicit note in
 `utils.py`'s own docstring that the shared filter's kept-name list is now load-bearing in **both** directions.
 
 - **Owner**: the content-coding posture pass — §RM-008's own owner, extended to the request direction; the two
   halves should be decided together so one filter does not carry two unreconciled postures.
 - **Trigger**: the first client that compresses a request body against this proxy (a field report, or a documented
   client adding it), **or** any change that arms a request-body cap again, **or** §RM-008 being taken up.
 - **Source**: `assumptions/compositions/S16-body-limit.md` §Notes (third bullet) and §What Is Required (a),
   re-read against `utils.py:20-26`, `routes/passthrough.py:14/31` and `buster_ripper_legacy.py` by
   [082-assumption-decide](../iterations/082-assumption-decide.md); `handoff-context.md` §Accepted risks
   (`S16-decompress`) and §Elevated to user (`Reported: —`).
 - **Source**: `CR3-F-11` via `iterations/079-codex-review.md`; `handoff-context.md` §Accepted risks
   (`CR3-F-11`) and §Elevated to user (`Reported: —`).
 
 ### RM-012: `/v1/messages` answers a generated 500 to a schema-invalid request body — DEFERRED (base-parity posture, `codify-scope`; routed 2026-10-07 by the CR4 closure-packet routing)
 
+**Status:** `promoted-onward` → `.sessions/codify/roadmap/2026-10-18-message-dump-bulk-flush.md` `RM-012`.
+
 Deferred: any `tools` or `messages` array carrying a **non-mapping element** — `{"tools":[null]}`,
 `{"messages":[null]}`, `{"messages":["x"]}` — raises `AttributeError` inside `normalize()`'s element loops
 (`normalize.py:75` reads `t.get("name","")`, `:117` reads `msg.get("content")`, and `:192` reads `msg.get("role")`
 under `STRIP_DATE` + sglang), the raise escapes `handle_messages`, and aiohttp authors the answer: a generated 500
 with a server-side traceback, where a proxy that validates input would author a 400. Reach: `latent` — exposed route,
 no guard prevents the input; malformed per the Anthropic schema, so the documented client never sends it.
 
 Why this is a deferral and not a wave member: **base does exactly the same thing, at the same point in its own
 `normalize()`.** Measured at all three points by the routing pass — `evidence/cr4-probe-matrix.txt` §3 runs base
 (`72e3a1c6`) against a live upstream stub, so the 500s are attributable and the well-formed control's 200 proves the
 harness can succeed, and the direct `normalize()` table in the same section shows the same raise with no transport in
 the way; §1 shows the package matching base at the pre-range branch `6508941` **and** at `HEAD`. Nothing this session
 introduced needs restoring on this route, which is the whole of the distinction from `T018`: what `T018` absorbed was
 a rejection base never imposed, and what is declined here is a rejection base imposes today. Hardening the loops
 therefore *changes* `/v1/messages` — measured **500 → 502** with the upstream unreachable (§7), i.e. the request now
 survives normalization and reaches the relay — and `G-003` holds that route's behavior still, its constraint column
 reading "No new endpoints, **no normalization changes**". The remaining question is a posture, not a defect: what
 should this proxy say when the body is well-formed JSON and invalid schema? Three admissible answers, none ranked by
 any settled authority available to this session, all of them out of its scope.
 
 What the fix owes, so it is not re-derived later: **all three loops in one motion** — a two-loop repair is incomplete,
 because `:192` is masked by `:117` today and inherits the crash the moment `:117` stops being it (§6 measures exactly
 that promotion). **And the loops are not the granularity — there are five mapping-assuming read sites, not three**:
 `_normalize_tools` reads `t.get("name", "")` **three** times (`:88` the `original_names` comprehension, `:89` the
 `sorted()` key, `:90` the `sorted_names` comprehension), `_strip_cache_control_from_messages` once (`:130`), and
 `_normalize_date` once (`:205`). Measured, twice independently. `090-01-forward`'s evidence §D records the mechanism
 while executing its replay (c): guarding `:88`, `:89` and the `:130` loop still left `{"tools":[null]}` 500ing, "because
 `_normalize_tools` reads `t.get('name','')` a second time in `sorted_names`" — but the same report's §Concerns 1
 enumerated the tools reads as two and the total as four, which is the count this row carried. `090-01-yield` re-ran the
 mutation per-site and **corrected it**: `{"tools":[null]}` stays green at **500** until `:90` is guarded too, and turns
 500 → 502 only when all three tools reads are covered. So a repair stated as "three loops" is incomplete by two sites,
 and one stated as "four read sites" is incomplete by one. A
 `continue`-style guard must also cover `:131` (`"cache_control" in msg`, `TypeError` on `None`) and `:209`
 (`msg["content"] = …`), both masked today by the read above them; a substitution-style guard does not reach them.
 Coordinates here are post-`T019.002` bytes: the `+13` delegating entry shifted every line below it, so where the
 paragraph above reads `normalize.py:75`/`:117`/`:192`, those sites are `:88`/`:130`/`:205` now. Then: the answer shape decided rather than drifted — `400` with the repo's own
 `{"error": …}` envelope (`messages.py:33`, `chat.py:32`, `count_tokens.py:23-25` are the existing authored shapes),
 **or** tolerate-and-forward and let the upstream reject it, **or** validate and drop the offending element and log the
 change the way `normalize()` logs every other mutation. Whichever is chosen owes witnesses at `/v1/messages` **and**
 `/v1/chat/completions` for each of the three element classes, one witness that the session-id digest is unaffected
 (`session.py:93-98` reads `system` only), and a decision on whether `count_tokens` then returns to the full
 `normalize()` call — because `T019.002` deliberately narrows that route to the system-strip step, and its Done-when
 clause 5 pins `/v1/messages` at 500 with witnesses that go red the moment this posture changes without this row being
 taken up.
 
 - **Owner**: the route-input posture design pass — the same owner as §RM-001 (the route-handler restructure), since
   both decide what `/v1/messages`' handler does with a body it did not ask for; §RM-008/§RM-011's content-coding
   postures are the adjacent decisions on the same request/response boundary.
 - **Trigger**: any pass that takes up §RM-001, **or** a field report of a 500 from this proxy on a malformed body,
   **or** the first feature that makes schema-invalid input reachable (a client other than Claude Code, or an
   input-validation surface on the route).
 - **Source**: `CR4-F-01` (second half) via [088-codex-review](../iterations/088-codex-review.md), adjudicated at
   [089-assumption-decide](../iterations/089-assumption-decide.md); `handoff-context.md` §Accepted risks
   (`CR4-F-01` second half), §Elevated to user (`Reported: —`), and `DC-CR4-F-01-r1`'s rejected-candidate list.
+

 """HTTP helper utilities shared across route handlers."""
 
 import json
 import logging
+from collections.abc import Mapping
 from typing import Optional
 
-from multidict import CIMultiDict, CIMultiDictProxy
+from multidict import CIMultiDict
 
 from . import config
 
 log = logging.getLogger("buster-ripper")
 
 
 _HOP_BY_HOP = frozenset([
     "connection", "keep-alive", "proxy-authenticate", "proxy-authorization",
     "te", "trailers", "transfer-encoding", "upgrade",
 ])
 
 
 def forward_headers(headers: dict) -> dict:
     """Build headers to send upstream, dropping hop-by-hop and host."""
     return {
         k: v
         for k, v in headers.items()
         if k.lower() not in ("host", "content-length", "connection", "transfer-encoding", *_HOP_BY_HOP)
     }
 
 
-def response_headers(upstream_headers: dict | CIMultiDictProxy) -> CIMultiDict:
+def response_headers(upstream_headers: Mapping[str, str]) -> CIMultiDict:
     """Build headers to send downstream, dropping hop-by-hop and content-length.
 
     One pair per *occurrence*: the client hands the upstream's head over as a
     per-occurrence mapping and ``web.Response`` writes one field-line per entry it is
     given, so a name the upstream sent twice (`Set-Cookie: a=1` then `b=2`, two `Vary`
     values) reaches the client twice. Folding into a plain ``dict`` here kept the last
     occurrence and destroyed the rest — and that was not base parity either, because
     base's client merged occurrences into one comma-joined value, so no value was ever
     lost on the way through.
 
     content-length is dropped alongside the hop-by-hop names because aiohttp
     re-derives it from the body we actually send: an upstream that answers with
     both Transfer-Encoding: chunked and a Content-Length would otherwise have its
     (lying) length relayed, since Response._start only derives a length when the
     header is absent.
 
     Kept names include ``Content-Encoding``, and this filter does not reconcile it with
     the body it travels beside: the package's client defaults to ``auto_decompress=True``
     and never overrides it, so the upstream's encoding label is relayed over bytes this
     proxy already decoded. That posture is base parity and stays as it is until the
     design pass rules on the alternative shapes — see ``roadmap.md`` §RM-008.
     """
     return CIMultiDict(
         (k, v)
         for k, v in upstream_headers.items()
         if k.lower() not in ("content-length", *_HOP_BY_HOP)
     )
 
 
 def extract_input_tokens(response_body: bytes) -> Optional[int]:
     """Extract input_tokens from a non-streaming JSON response body."""
     try:
         return json.loads(response_body).get("usage", {}).get("input_tokens")
     except Exception:
         return None
 
 
 def split_thinking(content: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
     """Parse a <think>...</think> block out of raw content.
 
     Some models (e.g. GLM-4.7 with enable_thinking=true) return the full
     generation in the content field:
         thinking reasoning text</think>actual answer
     vLLM may or may not split this into reasoning_content + content depending
     on the version and model. This function normalizes it so callers always get
     a clean (thinking, answer) pair regardless of what vLLM did.
 
     Returns (thinking, answer):
       - thinking: text inside the first <think>...</think> block, or ""
       - answer:   everything after </think>, stripped of leading whitespace,
                   or the full content unchanged if no <think> block is found
     """
     open_tag = content.find("<think>")
     close_tag = content.find("</think>")
     if open_tag == -1 or close_tag == -1 or close_tag < open_tag:
         return "", content
     thinking = content[open_tag + len("<think>"):close_tag]
     answer = content[close_tag + len("</think>"):].lstrip("\n")
     return thinking, answer

 #!/usr/bin/env python3
+# Tracked reference copy of the pre-refactor monolith, kept as the base-contract
+# authority for review lanes. It is not part of the buster_ripper package, must not
+# be imported or updated, and its scope exclusion is recorded in the session plan
+# (RB-002 lineage: "excluded from scope, deliberately not updated").
 # /// script
 # requires-python = ">=3.11"
 # dependencies = [
 #   "fastapi",
 #   "uvicorn[standard]",
 #   "httpx",
 #   "click",
 # ]
 # ///
 # ruff: noqa: E501
 """
 buster-ripper — Anthropic /v1/messages normalizing proxy for vLLM prefix-cache stability.
 
 Rips out the cache busters that Claude Code injects into every request,
 keeping the KV cache prefix stable across turns.
 
 Normalizations applied before forwarding to vLLM:
 
   1. Sort tool definitions alphabetically by name.
      GLM-4.7's chat template injects tools at position 0 of the formatted
      prompt (before the system message). Any ordering change invalidates the
      entire KV cache. MCP servers reconnect and return tools in arbitrary
      order — sorting makes the tool block byte-identical across turns.
 
   2. (Optional, --strip-date) Remove the Claude Code framework-injected
      "Today's date is YYYY-MM-DD." line from user message content.
      This is appended to MEMORY.md content inside <system-reminder> blocks
      and changes daily, busting the cache at midnight. Pass --strip-date to
      strip it; omit to leave it (model may use date awareness).
 
   3. (Optional, --dump-dir PATH) Session-aware prompt diffing.
      Saves the full normalized request body for turn 0 of each session, then
      a unified diff vs the previous turn for all subsequent turns.  Useful for
      identifying remaining cache busters after normalization.
 
      Session ID = first 12 hex chars of SHA-256(system_prompt_text).
      Stable within a Claude Code session; changes between sessions.
 
      Output layout:
        PATH/<session_id>/turn_000.json   — full body (turn 0)
        PATH/<session_id>/turn_001.diff   — unified diff vs turn 000
        PATH/<session_id>/turn_002.diff   — unified diff vs turn 001
        ...
        PATH/<session_id>/index.txt       — one-line summary per turn
 
   4. /v1/messages/count_tokens — Claude Code calls this to decide when to
      auto-compact the context. vLLM returns 404, so compaction never fires.
      We synthesize the response from tracked session usage. When both token
      fill AND TTFT latency exceed thresholds, we inflate the reported count
      to the nudge value, triggering Claude Code to compact and restore speed.
 
 Usage:
   uv run buster_ripper.py [--upstream URL] [--port PORT] [--strip-date] [--verbose]
                           [--dump-dir PATH] [--max-model-len N]
                           [--compact-token-ratio F] [--compact-latency-ms F]
                           [--stats-db PATH]
 
   # default: listen on 0.0.0.0:30001, forward to localhost:30000
   uv run buster_ripper.py

 def documented_files(n: int) -> set[str]:
     """Today's layout for a session of ``n`` turns: ``N + 1`` files."""
     return {"turn_000.json", "index.txt"} | {f"turn_{i:03d}.diff" for i in range(1, n)}
 
 # The published reader contract. README.md §Reading a collapsed artifact documents the
 # pattern a human/grep consumer matches dump lines with; §Reading the index documents the
 # columns. These are the only sources for "today's line format" a black-box gate may use.
 README_LINE_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^turn [0-9]{3}  [0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}")
 FULL_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\bFULL\b")
 KB_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\b\d+(?:\.\d+)? KB\b")
 CHANGE_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\+\d+/-\d+ lines\b")
 MSGS_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\b\d+ msgs\b")
 TOOLS_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\b\d+ tools\b")

 @pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
 def no_dump_config_override(monkeypatch):
     """``VP-T002-default-encoding`` requires *no config override*, not merely no CLI flag."""
     for name in [k for k in os.environ if "DUMP" in k.upper()]:
         monkeypatch.delenv(name, raising=False)
 
 
 def marker(tag: str, i: int) -> str:
     return f"{tag}-t{i}-uniq{int(time.monotonic() * 1000) % 100000:05d}-{i}"
 
 
 def nonce(tag: str, i: int) -> str:
     """A per-request billing nonce (``README.md`` §Normalizations 1), unique per turn."""
     return f"qa-nonce-{tag}-{i}-{int(time.monotonic() * 1000) % 100000:05d}"
 
 
 def listing(path: str) -> list[str]:
     return sorted(os.listdir(path)) if os.path.isdir(path) else []
 
 
 def index_lines(sid_dir: str) -> list[str]:
     text = read_text(os.path.join(sid_dir, "index.txt"))
     return [ln for ln in text.splitlines() if ln.strip()]
 
 
 def production_diff_text(previous: str, current: str) -> str:
     """The documented writer's published diff text for two public renderings.
 
-    ``README.md:154`` publishes ``turn_NNN.diff`` as a unified diff. The writer
+    ``README.md:165`` publishes ``turn_NNN.diff`` as a unified diff. The writer
     splits both renderings with ``splitlines(keepends=True)``, asks
     ``difflib.unified_diff`` for ``lineterm=""``, and joins the yielded lines
     with ``""`` — so each control line runs into the line that follows it. The gate
     mirrors that public shape here from the renderings alone and reads both sides
     with one reader (:func:`diff_changed_lines`); it never calls the
     implementation's own renderer or diff helper.
     """
     return "".join(difflib.unified_diff(
         previous.splitlines(keepends=True),
         current.splitlines(keepends=True),
         fromfile="previous", tofile="current", lineterm="",
     ))
 
 
 def changed_lines(previous: str, current: str) -> list[str]:
-    """The ``-``/``+`` content lines of ``turn NNN-1 -> turn NNN``, per README.md:154."""
+    """The ``-``/``+`` content lines of ``turn NNN-1 -> turn NNN``, per README.md:165."""
     return diff_changed_lines(production_diff_text(previous, current))
 
 
 def diff_changed_lines(diff_text: str) -> list[str]:
     """The ``-``/``+`` content lines of the writer's ``""``-joined unified diff.
 
     The writer's ``lineterm=""`` join concatenates each ``---``/``+++``/``@@``
     control line with the line that follows it, so a hunk's first changed line can
     be glued to the ``@@`` header. Recovery is structural and symmetric: consume
     the diff's own leading file-header pair and each ``@@`` control line, then keep
     the content line that follows with its own ending. A removed/added content line
     whose own text begins with ``--``/``++`` is not mistaken for a file header,
     because only the leading header pair is control text.
     """
     return [ln for ln in _content_lines(diff_text) if ln and ln[0] in "+-"]
 
 
 def _content_lines(diff_text: str) -> list[str]:
     """Split the writer's ``""``-joined diff text back into content lines."""
     lines: list[str] = []
     rest = diff_text
     header = True  # only the diff's own leading file-header pair is control text
     while rest:
         if header and rest.startswith("--- "):
             end = rest.find("+++ ")
             rest = rest[end:] if end > 0 else ""
         elif header and rest.startswith("+++ "):
             end = rest.find("@@ ")
             rest = rest[end:] if end > 0 else ""
             header = False
         elif rest.startswith("@@ "):
             end = rest.find("@@", 2)
             rest = rest[end + 2:] if end >= 0 else ""
         else:
             end = rest.find("\n")
             if end < 0:
                 lines.append(rest)
                 rest = ""
             else:
                 lines.append(rest[:end + 1])
                 rest = rest[end + 1:]
     return lines
 
 
 def rendering_section(artifact_text: str, header: str) -> str:
     """One turn's full rendering from a collapsed artifact, separator removed.
 
     The artifact separates records with ``\\n\\n`` and closes the last record with
     ``\\n``; the rendering itself always ends with ``\\n``. Remove only those
     separator newlines — never ``.strip()``, which erases a trailing newline the
     production diff reports (``S21`` CE-3).
     """
     start = artifact_text.find(header) + len(header)
     section = record_section(artifact_text, header)
     assert section.startswith("\n"), (
         f"record {header!r} must follow its header's line terminator"
     )
     body = section[1:]
     separator = "\n" if start + len(section) == len(artifact_text) else "\n\n"
     assert body.endswith(separator), (
         f"record {header!r} is not followed by the artifact's record separator"
     )
     return body[: -len(separator)]
 
 
 def assert_documented_contents(
     sid_dir: str,
     markers: list[str],
     expected_body: dict | None = None,
     renderings: list[str] | None = None,
 ) -> None:
     """Every clause of ``VP-T002-documented-format-preserved``'s "today's content and line
     format", read off ``README.md`` §Dump mode — the public contract, not the implementation.
 
     ``expected_body`` is the **complete** normalized request body the gate expects in
-    ``turn_000.json`` (``README.md:153``: "full normalized request body"). ``renderings``
-    are the ``self-contained`` artifact's per-turn renderings (``README.md:188``), from which
-    the documented diff's source is derived (``README.md:154``). Both are supplied by the
+    ``turn_000.json`` (``README.md:164``: "full normalized request body"). ``renderings``
+    are the ``self-contained`` artifact's per-turn renderings (``README.md:199``), from which
+    the documented diff's source is derived (``README.md:165``). Both are supplied by the
     caller, so no expectation is produced by the code under test.
     """
     # turn_000.json — "full normalized request body (turn 0)"
     body = json.loads(read_text(os.path.join(sid_dir, "turn_000.json")))
     assert isinstance(body, dict) and body.get("model"), "turn_000.json is not a request body"
     assert markers[0] in json.dumps(body), "turn_000.json lost the turn's content"
     if expected_body is not None:
         missing = sorted(set(expected_body) - set(body))
         unexpected = sorted(set(body) - set(expected_body))
         differing = sorted(k for k in set(body) & set(expected_body) if body[k] != expected_body[k])
         assert body == expected_body, (
             "turn_000.json is not the normalized request body: "
             f"missing={missing} unexpected={unexpected} differing={differing}"
         )
 
     # index.txt — "one-line summary per turn", in the documented shape
     lines = index_lines(sid_dir)
     assert len(lines) == len(markers), f"index.txt must carry one line per turn: {lines!r}"
     for i, line in enumerate(lines):
         assert README_LINE_PATTERN.match(line), (
             f"index.txt line {i} does not match the README reader pattern "
             f"{README_LINE_PATTERN.pattern!r}: {line!r}"
         )
         cols = line.split()
         assert cols[0] == "turn" and cols[1] == f"{i:03d}", (
             f"documented line {i} lost its turn number: {line!r}"
         )
         assert MSGS_CLAUSE.search(line), f"documented line lost its msgs column: {line!r}"
         assert TOOLS_CLAUSE.search(line), f"documented line lost its tools column: {line!r}"
         if i == 0:
             assert FULL_CLAUSE.search(line), f"turn 000 lost the documented FULL marker: {line!r}"
             assert KB_CLAUSE.search(line), f"turn 000 lost the documented size column: {line!r}"
         else:
             assert CHANGE_CLAUSE.search(line), (
                 f"turn {i:03d} lost the documented +A/-R lines column: {line!r}"
             )
 
     # turn_NNN.diff — "unified diff: turn NNN-1 -> turn NNN". Raw read: the
     # writer computed this text before the write, over the renderings' own line
     # endings, so the comparison must not normalize them (S21 CE-2).
     for i in range(1, len(markers)):
         diff = read_text(os.path.join(sid_dir, f"turn_{i:03d}.diff"), newline="")
         assert "---" in diff and "+++" in diff and "@@" in diff, (
             f"turn_{i:03d}.diff is not a unified diff"
         )
         assert any(ln.startswith("+") and markers[i] in ln for ln in diff.splitlines()), (
             f"turn_{i:03d}.diff does not add the turn's own content"
         )
         assert any(ln.startswith("-") and markers[i - 1] in ln for ln in diff.splitlines()), (
             f"turn_{i:03d}.diff does not remove the previous turn's content"
         )
         if renderings is not None:
             expected = changed_lines(renderings[i - 1], renderings[i])
             observed = diff_changed_lines(diff)
             assert observed == expected, (
                 f"turn_{i:03d}.diff is not computed over the turn's rendering: "
                 f"expected={expected!r} observed={observed!r}"
             )

 def test_tc108_documented_format_preserves_contents_at_2n_writes(tmp_path):

     )

         "turn_001.diff": 1,

 """Instrumented proxy entry for the upstream-TLS posture gates (TC-114 evidence).
 
 Runs the real CLI in a real process. Only ``aiohttp``'s public client
 constructors are wrapped, to answer the one question ``qa-plan.md`` assigns to
 this tier — the plan's own words: *"exactly one `ClientSession` exists ... so
 one connector governs every outbound call"* and its evidence source, *"one
 connector assertion + the instrumented call sites"*. Nothing in the proxy is
 patched or reordered; the posture itself is proven black-box by the loopback
 peer's request log, and these counts only establish that a single client object
 could have served everything the peer saw.
 
 Usage::
 
     python tls_probe_entry.py --report PATH -- <proxy args>
 
 At process exit a JSON report is written::
 
     {"connectors": [{"index", "id", "ssl_kwarg", "positionals", "kwargs"}],
      "sessions":   [{"index", "id", "connector_kwarg", "ssl_kwarg", "connector_id"}],
      "calls":      [{"method", "path", "session_id", "connector_id"}],
      "total_connectors": int, "total_sessions": int, "total_calls": int,
      "call_hook": "installed" | "unavailable"}
 """
 
 from __future__ import annotations
 
 import argparse
 import json
-import os
 import sys
 
 connectors: list[dict] = []
 sessions: list[dict] = []
 calls: list[dict] = []
 _ABSENT = "<absent>"
 
 
 def _posture(kwargs: dict):
     """How the caller spelled the ssl posture, or a marker that it did not."""
     return repr(kwargs["ssl"]) if "ssl" in kwargs else _ABSENT
 
 
 def _main() -> int:
     argv = sys.argv[1:]
     try:
         sep = argv.index("--")
     except ValueError:
         raise SystemExit("usage: tls_probe_entry.py --report PATH -- <proxy args>")
     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
     parser.add_argument("--report", required=True)
     args = parser.parse_args(argv[:sep])
     proxy_argv = argv[sep + 1:]
 
     import aiohttp
 
     real_connector_init = aiohttp.TCPConnector.__init__
     real_session_init = aiohttp.ClientSession.__init__
 
     def connector_init(self, *init_args, **init_kwargs):
         real_connector_init(self, *init_args, **init_kwargs)
         connectors.append(
             {
                 "index": len(connectors),
                 "id": id(self),
                 # The plan settles the construction as TCPConnector(ssl=...);
                 # ssl is keyword-only there, and the positionals are recorded so
                 # a posture spelled another way is still visible in the report.
                 "ssl_kwarg": _posture(init_kwargs),
                 "positionals": [repr(a)[:80] for a in init_args],
                 "kwargs": sorted(init_kwargs),
             }
         )
 
     def session_init(self, *init_args, **init_kwargs):
         real_session_init(self, *init_args, **init_kwargs)
         connector = getattr(self, "connector", None)
         sessions.append(
             {
                 "index": len(sessions),
                 "id": id(self),
                 "connector_kwarg": "connector" in init_kwargs,
                 "ssl_kwarg": _posture(init_kwargs),
                 "connector_id": id(connector) if connector is not None else None,
             }
         )
 
     aiohttp.TCPConnector.__init__ = connector_init
     aiohttp.ClientSession.__init__ = session_init

 class TestMessagesMalformedMemberPosture:

         Green today because the raise happens in `normalize()` before the relay is
         reached. Hardening the loops lets the request through, so the upstream call is
         made and the answer becomes a 502 — base's authority for this route is
         `buster_ripper.py:374`/`:402`, which raise the identical `AttributeError`, and
         `roadmap.md` §RM-012 owns any change to that posture.
 
         The upstream call is stubbed to refuse rather than pointed at a socket, so the
         502 arm this witness describes is the same one `T013`'s review accepted.
         """
         mocker.patch.dict(session_mod._stats, {}, clear=True)
         sent = mocker.patch.object(proxy.server.app["session"], "post",
                                    side_effect=aiohttp.ClientConnectionError("stub refused"))
 
         response = await proxy.post(_MESSAGES, json=body)
 
         assert response.status == 500, (
             "/v1/messages stopped answering base's 500 — see roadmap.md §RM-012")
         assert not sent.called, "the request survived normalization and reached the relay"
 
     async def test_a_well_formed_body_still_reaches_the_relay(self, proxy, mocker):
         """The pair that keeps the witness above from passing on a dead route.
 
         Same stub, opposite expectation: a body normalization accepts must reach the
         upstream call and relay its answer, so a 500 in the class above is attributable
         to `normalize()` rather than to a route that never forwards anything.
         """

     def test_utf8_pending_bytes_are_session_local(self, dump_env, fmt, text):
         """Each session counts rendered UTF-8 bytes, not records or characters."""
         config.DUMP_FORMAT = fmt
         a = _body("sys-a", [text])
         b = _body("sys-b", ["other"])
         dump_turn(a)
         dump_turn(b)
 
         state_a = dump._sessions[session_id(a)]
         state_b = dump._sessions[session_id(b)]
         assert state_a.pending_bytes == len(_render_body(a).encode("utf-8"))
         assert state_b.pending_bytes == len(_render_body(b).encode("utf-8"))
         dump.flush_all()
         assert state_a.pending_bytes == state_b.pending_bytes == 0
         assert len(state_a.pending) == len(state_b.pending) == 0
 
     @pytest.mark.parametrize("fmt", COLLAPSED_FORMATS)
     def test_state_advances_at_append_time(self, dump_env, fmt):
         """turn/prev_text advance on every call, together with the buffer."""
         config.DUMP_FORMAT = fmt
         bodies = _turn_bodies("sys-a", 3)
         sid = session_id(bodies[0])
         for i, body in enumerate(bodies):
             dump_turn(body)
             state = dump._sessions[sid]
             assert state.turn == i + 1
             assert state.prev_text == _render_body(body)
             assert len(state.pending) == i + 1
 
 
 class TestDocumentedRunWitness:
     """claim-93's documented half, as one fixed-format run.
 
     The format is never changed between turns: documented turns write their
     files immediately, the pending list stays empty, and no collapsed .log
     artifact is ever created.
     """
 
     def test_immediate_files_empty_pending_no_log(self, dump_env):
         """Each turn lands on disk at once; nothing buffers; no .log exists."""
         bodies = _turn_bodies("sys-a", 3)
         sid = session_id(bodies[0])
         for i, body in enumerate(bodies):
             dump_turn(body)
             state = dump._sessions[sid]
             expected = f"turn_{i:03d}.json" if i == 0 else f"turn_{i:03d}.diff"
             assert (dump_env / sid / expected).is_file()
             assert state.pending == []
             assert state.pending_bytes == 0
 
         assert len((dump_env / sid / "index.txt").read_text().splitlines()) == 3
         assert list((dump_env / sid).glob("*.log")) == []

 def test_billing_nonce_stripped_from_system_list():
     body = {
         "system": [
             {"type": "text", "text": "x-anthropic-billing-header: cc_version=2.1.50; cch=abc123;\nReal system prompt."},
         ],
         "messages": [],
     }
     out, changes = normalize(body)
     assert out["system"][0]["text"] == "Real system prompt."
     assert any("billing" in c for c in changes)
 
 
 def test_billing_nonce_only_block_removed():
     """A system block containing only the billing nonce is dropped entirely."""
     body = {
         "system": [
             {"type": "text", "text": "x-anthropic-billing-header: cc_version=2.1.50; cch=abc123;\n"},
             {"type": "text", "text": "Keep this."},
         ],
         "messages": [],
     }
     out, changes = normalize(body)
     assert len(out["system"]) == 1
     assert out["system"][0]["text"] == "Keep this."
     assert any("billing" in c for c in changes)
 
 
 def test_billing_nonce_stripped_from_system_string():
     body = {
         "system": "x-anthropic-billing-header: cch=xyz;\nSystem prompt here.",
         "messages": [],
     }
     out, changes = normalize(body)
     assert out["system"] == "System prompt here."
     assert any("billing" in c for c in changes)

     body = {"system": [{"type": "text", "text": "Clean system prompt."}], "messages": []}
     out, changes = normalize(body)
     assert out["system"][0]["text"] == "Clean system prompt."

 # ── SessionStats.update ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 
 
 def test_first_update_sets_latency_directly():
     s = SessionStats()
     s.update(input_tokens=1000, latency_ms=5000.0)
     assert s.input_tokens == 1000
     assert s.avg_latency_ms == 5000.0
     assert s.request_count == 1
 
 
 def test_second_update_applies_ema():
     s = SessionStats()
     s.update(1000, 5000.0)
     s.update(2000, 10000.0, ema_alpha=0.3)
     assert s.input_tokens == 2000
     assert s.avg_latency_ms == pytest.approx(6500.0)
     assert s.request_count == 2
 
 
 def test_ema_smooths_spike():
     s = SessionStats()
     for _ in range(5):
         s.update(50_000, 3000.0)
     assert s.avg_latency_ms < 5000
 
     s.update(50_000, 30_000.0)
     assert s.avg_latency_ms < 15_000
 
 
 def test_input_tokens_always_latest():
     s = SessionStats()
     s.update(100_000, 1000.0)
     s.update(120_000, 2000.0)
     s.update(50_000, 1000.0)
     assert s.input_tokens == 50_000

             assert "skip_tls_verify" not in ctx.params
         finally:

         """No flag restores the off default over any prior mutation."""

         ctx = main.make_context("buster-ripper", [])
         try:
             assert config.SKIP_TLS_VERIFY is False
             assert "skip_tls_verify" not in ctx.params
         finally:
             ctx.close()

 class TestNoRequestBodyCap:

                      id="passthrough-catch-all"),
     ])
     async def test_a_two_mebibyte_body_reaches_the_upstream(self, proxy, mocker, case):
         """Red at pre-fix bytes as a 413 out of aiohttp's reader, before any handler
         ran: the cap is the application's, so it is one construction site to change."""
         path, kwargs, method = case
         sent = mocker.patch.object(
             proxy.server.app["session"], method,
             return_value=StubUpstream(body=_ANSWER, headers=_JSON))
 
         response = await proxy.request("POST", path, **kwargs)
 
         assert response.status == 200, "the app answered for the body size"
         assert sent.called, "the read never happened, so nothing was forwarded"

                                     json=dict(_CHAT, max_tokens=10))
 
         assert response.status == 200, "the app answered for the body size"
         assert "input_tokens" in await response.json()

         because nothing is armed on this call."""

     async def test_the_streaming_call_passes_a_timeout_with_nothing_armed(self, mocker,
                                                                          raw_proxy):
         """All four components unset, not merely a ``total`` that looks unlimited: the
         request-local object replaces the session's wholesale, so a spelling that
         re-inherited ``total`` — or armed ``sock_read`` "to be safe" — would pass the
