 """HTTP helper utilities shared across route handlers."""
 
 import json
 import logging
+from collections.abc import Mapping
 from typing import Optional
 
-from multidict import CIMultiDict, CIMultiDictProxy
+from multidict import CIMultiDict
 
 from . import config
 
 log = logging.getLogger("buster-ripper")
 
 
 _HOP_BY_HOP = frozenset([
     "connection", "keep-alive", "proxy-authenticate", "proxy-authorization",
     "te", "trailers", "transfer-encoding", "upgrade",
 ])
 
 
 def forward_headers(headers: dict) -> dict:
     """Build headers to send upstream, dropping hop-by-hop and host."""
     return {
         k: v
         for k, v in headers.items()
         if k.lower() not in ("host", "content-length", "connection", "transfer-encoding", *_HOP_BY_HOP)
     }
 
 
-def response_headers(upstream_headers: dict | CIMultiDictProxy) -> CIMultiDict:
+def response_headers(upstream_headers: Mapping[str, str]) -> CIMultiDict:
     """Build headers to send downstream, dropping hop-by-hop and content-length.
 
     One pair per *occurrence*: the client hands the upstream's head over as a
     per-occurrence mapping and ``web.Response`` writes one field-line per entry it is
     given, so a name the upstream sent twice (`Set-Cookie: a=1` then `b=2`, two `Vary`
     values) reaches the client twice. Folding into a plain ``dict`` here kept the last
     occurrence and destroyed the rest — and that was not base parity either, because
     base's client merged occurrences into one comma-joined value, so no value was ever
     lost on the way through.
 
     content-length is dropped alongside the hop-by-hop names because aiohttp
     re-derives it from the body we actually send: an upstream that answers with
     both Transfer-Encoding: chunked and a Content-Length would otherwise have its
     (lying) length relayed, since Response._start only derives a length when the
     header is absent.
 
     Kept names include ``Content-Encoding``, and this filter does not reconcile it with
     the body it travels beside: the package's client defaults to ``auto_decompress=True``
     and never overrides it, so the upstream's encoding label is relayed over bytes this
     proxy already decoded. That posture is base parity and stays as it is until the
     design pass rules on the alternative shapes — see ``roadmap.md`` §RM-008.
     """
     return CIMultiDict(
         (k, v)
         for k, v in upstream_headers.items()
         if k.lower() not in ("content-length", *_HOP_BY_HOP)
     )
 
 
 def extract_input_tokens(response_body: bytes) -> Optional[int]:
     """Extract input_tokens from a non-streaming JSON response body."""
     try:
         return json.loads(response_body).get("usage", {}).get("input_tokens")
     except Exception:
         return None
 
 
 def split_thinking(content: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
     """Parse a <think>...</think> block out of raw content.
 
     Some models (e.g. GLM-4.7 with enable_thinking=true) return the full
     generation in the content field:
         thinking reasoning text</think>actual answer
     vLLM may or may not split this into reasoning_content + content depending
     on the version and model. This function normalizes it so callers always get
     a clean (thinking, answer) pair regardless of what vLLM did.
 
     Returns (thinking, answer):
       - thinking: text inside the first <think>...</think> block, or ""
       - answer:   everything after </think>, stripped of leading whitespace,
                   or the full content unchanged if no <think> block is found
     """
     open_tag = content.find("<think>")
     close_tag = content.find("</think>")
     if open_tag == -1 or close_tag == -1 or close_tag < open_tag:
         return "", content
     thinking = content[open_tag + len("<think>"):close_tag]
     answer = content[close_tag + len("</think>"):].lstrip("\n")
     return thinking, answer
 
 
 def find_message_start(chunk: bytes) -> Optional[dict]:
     """Scan an SSE chunk for a 'message_start' event. Returns parsed dict or None.
 
     The message_start event carries usage.input_tokens and is always the first
     SSE event in an Anthropic streaming response — it arrives in the first
     network chunk, making it a reliable TTFT signal.
     """
     text = chunk.decode(errors="replace")
     for line in text.splitlines():
         if not line.startswith("data: "):
             continue
         payload = line[6:].strip()
         if payload == "[DONE]":
             continue
         try:
             evt = json.loads(payload)
             if evt.get("type") == "message_start":
                 return evt
         except Exception:

 #!/usr/bin/env python3
+# Tracked reference copy of the pre-refactor monolith, kept as the base-contract
+# authority for review lanes. It is not part of the buster_ripper package, must not
+# be imported or updated, and its scope exclusion is recorded in the session plan
+# (RB-002 lineage: "excluded from scope, deliberately not updated").
 # /// script
 # requires-python = ">=3.11"
 # dependencies = [
 #   "fastapi",
 #   "uvicorn[standard]",
 #   "httpx",
 #   "click",
 # ]
 # ///
 # ruff: noqa: E501
 """
 buster-ripper — Anthropic /v1/messages normalizing proxy for vLLM prefix-cache stability.
 
 Rips out the cache busters that Claude Code injects into every request,
 keeping the KV cache prefix stable across turns.
 
 Normalizations applied before forwarding to vLLM:
 
   1. Sort tool definitions alphabetically by name.
      GLM-4.7's chat template injects tools at position 0 of the formatted
      prompt (before the system message). Any ordering change invalidates the
      entire KV cache. MCP servers reconnect and return tools in arbitrary
      order — sorting makes the tool block byte-identical across turns.
 
   2. (Optional, --strip-date) Remove the Claude Code framework-injected
      "Today's date is YYYY-MM-DD." line from user message content.
      This is appended to MEMORY.md content inside <system-reminder> blocks
      and changes daily, busting the cache at midnight. Pass --strip-date to
      strip it; omit to leave it (model may use date awareness).
 
   3. (Optional, --dump-dir PATH) Session-aware prompt diffing.
      Saves the full normalized request body for turn 0 of each session, then
      a unified diff vs the previous turn for all subsequent turns.  Useful for
      identifying remaining cache busters after normalization.
 
      Session ID = first 12 hex chars of SHA-256(system_prompt_text).
      Stable within a Claude Code session; changes between sessions.
 
      Output layout:
        PATH/<session_id>/turn_000.json   — full body (turn 0)
        PATH/<session_id>/turn_001.diff   — unified diff vs turn 000
        PATH/<session_id>/turn_002.diff   — unified diff vs turn 001
        ...
        PATH/<session_id>/index.txt       — one-line summary per turn
 
   4. /v1/messages/count_tokens — Claude Code calls this to decide when to
      auto-compact the context. vLLM returns 404, so compaction never fires.
      We synthesize the response from tracked session usage. When both token
      fill AND TTFT latency exceed thresholds, we inflate the reported count
      to the nudge value, triggering Claude Code to compact and restore speed.
 
 Usage:
   uv run buster_ripper.py [--upstream URL] [--port PORT] [--strip-date] [--verbose]
                           [--dump-dir PATH] [--max-model-len N]
                           [--compact-token-ratio F] [--compact-latency-ms F]
                           [--stats-db PATH]
 
   # default: listen on 0.0.0.0:30001, forward to localhost:30000
   uv run buster_ripper.py
 
   # enable prompt diffing
   uv run buster_ripper.py --dump-dir ~/mllm/prompt-diffs
 
 Point Claude Code at buster-ripper:
   ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://localhost:30001 claude ...
 """
 
 import asyncio
 import click
 import dataclasses
 import difflib
 import hashlib
 import json
 import logging
 import re
 import sqlite3
 import time
 from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
 from datetime import datetime, timezone
 from pathlib import Path

 def documented_files(n: int) -> set[str]:
     """Today's layout for a session of ``n`` turns: ``N + 1`` files."""
     return {"turn_000.json", "index.txt"} | {f"turn_{i:03d}.diff" for i in range(1, n)}
 
 # The published reader contract. README.md §Reading a collapsed artifact documents the
 # pattern a human/grep consumer matches dump lines with; §Reading the index documents the
 # columns. These are the only sources for "today's line format" a black-box gate may use.
 README_LINE_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^turn [0-9]{3}  [0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}")
 FULL_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\bFULL\b")
 KB_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\b\d+(?:\.\d+)? KB\b")
 CHANGE_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\+\d+/-\d+ lines\b")
 MSGS_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\b\d+ msgs\b")
 TOOLS_CLAUSE = re.compile(r"\b\d+ tools\b")

 @pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
 def no_dump_config_override(monkeypatch):
     """``VP-T002-default-encoding`` requires *no config override*, not merely no CLI flag."""
     for name in [k for k in os.environ if "DUMP" in k.upper()]:
         monkeypatch.delenv(name, raising=False)
 
 
 def marker(tag: str, i: int) -> str:
     return f"{tag}-t{i}-uniq{int(time.monotonic() * 1000) % 100000:05d}-{i}"
 
 
 def nonce(tag: str, i: int) -> str:
     """A per-request billing nonce (``README.md`` §Normalizations 1), unique per turn."""
     return f"qa-nonce-{tag}-{i}-{int(time.monotonic() * 1000) % 100000:05d}"
 
 
 def listing(path: str) -> list[str]:
     return sorted(os.listdir(path)) if os.path.isdir(path) else []
 
 
 def index_lines(sid_dir: str) -> list[str]:
     text = read_text(os.path.join(sid_dir, "index.txt"))
     return [ln for ln in text.splitlines() if ln.strip()]
 
 
 def production_diff_text(previous: str, current: str) -> str:
     """The documented writer's published diff text for two public renderings.
 
-    ``README.md:154`` publishes ``turn_NNN.diff`` as a unified diff. The writer
+    ``README.md:165`` publishes ``turn_NNN.diff`` as a unified diff. The writer
     splits both renderings with ``splitlines(keepends=True)``, asks
     ``difflib.unified_diff`` for ``lineterm=""``, and joins the yielded lines
     with ``""`` — so each control line runs into the line that follows it. The gate
     mirrors that public shape here from the renderings alone and reads both sides
     with one reader (:func:`diff_changed_lines`); it never calls the
     implementation's own renderer or diff helper.
     """
     return "".join(difflib.unified_diff(
         previous.splitlines(keepends=True),
         current.splitlines(keepends=True),
         fromfile="previous", tofile="current", lineterm="",
     ))
 
 
 def changed_lines(previous: str, current: str) -> list[str]:
-    """The ``-``/``+`` content lines of ``turn NNN-1 -> turn NNN``, per README.md:154."""
+    """The ``-``/``+`` content lines of ``turn NNN-1 -> turn NNN``, per README.md:165."""
     return diff_changed_lines(production_diff_text(previous, current))
 
 
 def diff_changed_lines(diff_text: str) -> list[str]:
     """The ``-``/``+`` content lines of the writer's ``""``-joined unified diff.
 
     The writer's ``lineterm=""`` join concatenates each ``---``/``+++``/``@@``
     control line with the line that follows it, so a hunk's first changed line can
     be glued to the ``@@`` header. Recovery is structural and symmetric: consume
     the diff's own leading file-header pair and each ``@@`` control line, then keep
     the content line that follows with its own ending. A removed/added content line
     whose own text begins with ``--``/``++`` is not mistaken for a file header,
     because only the leading header pair is control text.
     """
     return [ln for ln in _content_lines(diff_text) if ln and ln[0] in "+-"]
 
 
 def _content_lines(diff_text: str) -> list[str]:
     """Split the writer's ``""``-joined diff text back into content lines."""
     lines: list[str] = []
     rest = diff_text
     header = True  # only the diff's own leading file-header pair is control text
     while rest:
         if header and rest.startswith("--- "):
             end = rest.find("+++ ")
             rest = rest[end:] if end > 0 else ""
         elif header and rest.startswith("+++ "):
             end = rest.find("@@ ")
             rest = rest[end:] if end > 0 else ""
             header = False
         elif rest.startswith("@@ "):
             end = rest.find("@@", 2)
             rest = rest[end + 2:] if end >= 0 else ""
         else:
             end = rest.find("\n")
             if end < 0:
                 lines.append(rest)
                 rest = ""
             else:
                 lines.append(rest[:end + 1])
                 rest = rest[end + 1:]
     return lines
 
 
 def rendering_section(artifact_text: str, header: str) -> str:
     """One turn's full rendering from a collapsed artifact, separator removed.
 
     The artifact separates records with ``\\n\\n`` and closes the last record with
     ``\\n``; the rendering itself always ends with ``\\n``. Remove only those
     separator newlines — never ``.strip()``, which erases a trailing newline the
     production diff reports (``S21`` CE-3).
     """
     start = artifact_text.find(header) + len(header)
     section = record_section(artifact_text, header)
     assert section.startswith("\n"), (
         f"record {header!r} must follow its header's line terminator"
     )
     body = section[1:]
     separator = "\n" if start + len(section) == len(artifact_text) else "\n\n"
     assert body.endswith(separator), (
         f"record {header!r} is not followed by the artifact's record separator"
     )
     return body[: -len(separator)]
 
 
 def assert_documented_contents(
     sid_dir: str,
     markers: list[str],
     expected_body: dict | None = None,
     renderings: list[str] | None = None,
 ) -> None:
     """Every clause of ``VP-T002-documented-format-preserved``'s "today's content and line
     format", read off ``README.md`` §Dump mode — the public contract, not the implementation.
 
     ``expected_body`` is the **complete** normalized request body the gate expects in
-    ``turn_000.json`` (``README.md:153``: "full normalized request body"). ``renderings``
-    are the ``self-contained`` artifact's per-turn renderings (``README.md:188``), from which
-    the documented diff's source is derived (``README.md:154``). Both are supplied by the
+    ``turn_000.json`` (``README.md:164``: "full normalized request body"). ``renderings``
+    are the ``self-contained`` artifact's per-turn renderings (``README.md:199``), from which
+    the documented diff's source is derived (``README.md:165``). Both are supplied by the
     caller, so no expectation is produced by the code under test.
     """
     # turn_000.json — "full normalized request body (turn 0)"
     body = json.loads(read_text(os.path.join(sid_dir, "turn_000.json")))
     assert isinstance(body, dict) and body.get("model"), "turn_000.json is not a request body"
     assert markers[0] in json.dumps(body), "turn_000.json lost the turn's content"
     if expected_body is not None:
         missing = sorted(set(expected_body) - set(body))
         unexpected = sorted(set(body) - set(expected_body))
         differing = sorted(k for k in set(body) & set(expected_body) if body[k] != expected_body[k])
         assert body == expected_body, (
             "turn_000.json is not the normalized request body: "
             f"missing={missing} unexpected={unexpected} differing={differing}"
         )
 
     # index.txt — "one-line summary per turn", in the documented shape
     lines = index_lines(sid_dir)
     assert len(lines) == len(markers), f"index.txt must carry one line per turn: {lines!r}"
     for i, line in enumerate(lines):
         assert README_LINE_PATTERN.match(line), (
             f"index.txt line {i} does not match the README reader pattern "
             f"{README_LINE_PATTERN.pattern!r}: {line!r}"
         )
         cols = line.split()
         assert cols[0] == "turn" and cols[1] == f"{i:03d}", (
             f"documented line {i} lost its turn number: {line!r}"
         )
         assert MSGS_CLAUSE.search(line), f"documented line lost its msgs column: {line!r}"
         assert TOOLS_CLAUSE.search(line), f"documented line lost its tools column: {line!r}"
         if i == 0:
             assert FULL_CLAUSE.search(line), f"turn 000 lost the documented FULL marker: {line!r}"
             assert KB_CLAUSE.search(line), f"turn 000 lost the documented size column: {line!r}"
         else:
             assert CHANGE_CLAUSE.search(line), (
                 f"turn {i:03d} lost the documented +A/-R lines column: {line!r}"
             )
 
     # turn_NNN.diff — "unified diff: turn NNN-1 -> turn NNN". Raw read: the
     # writer computed this text before the write, over the renderings' own line
     # endings, so the comparison must not normalize them (S21 CE-2).
     for i in range(1, len(markers)):
         diff = read_text(os.path.join(sid_dir, f"turn_{i:03d}.diff"), newline="")
         assert "---" in diff and "+++" in diff and "@@" in diff, (
             f"turn_{i:03d}.diff is not a unified diff"
         )
         assert any(ln.startswith("+") and markers[i] in ln for ln in diff.splitlines()), (
             f"turn_{i:03d}.diff does not add the turn's own content"
         )
         assert any(ln.startswith("-") and markers[i - 1] in ln for ln in diff.splitlines()), (
             f"turn_{i:03d}.diff does not remove the previous turn's content"
         )
         if renderings is not None:
             expected = changed_lines(renderings[i - 1], renderings[i])
             observed = diff_changed_lines(diff)
             assert observed == expected, (
                 f"turn_{i:03d}.diff is not computed over the turn's rendering: "
                 f"expected={expected!r} observed={observed!r}"
             )
 
 
 def count_report(path: str) -> dict:
     with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
         return json.load(f)
 
 
 def ops_by_name(report: dict) -> dict[str, int]:
     out: dict[str, int] = {}
     for op in report["ops"]:
         out[os.path.basename(op["path"])] = out.get(os.path.basename(op["path"]), 0) + 1
     return out

 # TC-108 — VP-T002-documented-format-preserved: layout, contents, and the 2N count
 # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 
 def test_tc108_documented_format_preserves_contents_at_2n_writes(tmp_path):
     system = "qa-ms001-documented"
     dump = tmp_path / "dump"
     report_path = tmp_path / "report-tc108.json"
     proxy = Proxy(
         dump,
         tmp_path / "logs",
         extra_args=["--dump-format", "documented"],
         counted_report=report_path,
     )
     sid_dir = proxy.session_dir(system)
     markers = [marker("documented", i) for i in range(N_TURNS)]
     nonces = [nonce("documented", i) for i in range(N_TURNS)]
     bodies = [turn_body(system, m, nonce=n) for m, n in zip(markers, nonces)]
 
     # The rendering oracle. `self-contained` stores every turn's complete rendering
-    # (README.md:188), so a documented diff over consecutive renderings is derivable
+    # (README.md:199), so a documented diff over consecutive renderings is derivable
     # from a public artifact with no call into the implementation's renderer. Its own
     # dump root keeps this scenario's documented listing unambiguous.
     sc_dump = tmp_path / "dump-renderings"
     sc_proxy = Proxy(
         sc_dump,
         tmp_path / "logs-renderings",
         extra_args=["--dump-format", "self-contained"],
     )
     sc_sid_dir = sc_proxy.session_dir(system)
     try:
         assert listing(sid_dir) == [], "sensitivity: nothing on disk before the first turn"
         for body in bodies:
             assert_upstream_response(*proxy.post(body))
         for body in bodies:
             assert_upstream_response(*sc_proxy.post(body))
     finally:
         rc = proxy.stop()
         sc_rc = sc_proxy.stop()
     assert rc == 0
     assert sc_rc == 0
 
     assert listing(sid_dir) == sorted(documented_files(N_TURNS)), (
         f"today's layout is N + 1 files: {listing(sid_dir)!r}"
     )
     assert listing(sc_sid_dir) == ["self-contained.log"], (
         f"the rendering oracle must hold its one artifact: {listing(sc_sid_dir)!r}"
     )
     # Raw read: these renderings are the expected side of the diff comparison and
     # must carry the line endings production diffed before the write (S21 CE-2).
     sc_text = read_text(os.path.join(sc_sid_dir, "self-contained.log"), newline="")
     renderings = [
         rendering_section(sc_text, f"--- turn {i:03d} rendered ---")
         for i in range(N_TURNS)
     ]
     assert_documented_contents(
         sid_dir,
         markers,
         expected_body={**bodies[0], "system": system},
         renderings=renderings,
     )
     for n in nonces:
         for root in (sid_dir, sc_sid_dir):
             for name in listing(root):
                 assert n not in read_text(os.path.join(root, name)), (
                     f"the billing nonce survived into {name}: every writer must dump the "
                     "normalized body"
                 )
     assert log_artifacts(str(dump)) == [], "no .log artifact on the documented path"
 
     # The instrumented measure M1: 2N write-bearing operations, decomposed so a
     # rewrite-per-turn or a lost index write cannot hide behind the total.
     report = count_report(str(report_path))
     assert report["total_ops"] == 2 * N_TURNS, (
         f"documented format must cost 2N={2 * N_TURNS} write ops, got {report['total_ops']}: "
         f"{ops_by_name(report)}"
     )
     assert ops_by_name(report) == {
         "index.txt": N_TURNS,
         "turn_000.json": 1,
         "turn_001.diff": 1,
         "turn_002.diff": 1,
     }, f"per-path write decomposition drifted: {ops_by_name(report)}"
     root = os.path.abspath(str(sid_dir))
     assert all(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(o["path"])) == root for o in report["ops"]), (
         "a documented write escaped this session's directory"
     )
     assert sum(o["bytes"] for o in report["ops"]) > 0
     # The documented half of the G-008(b) pair (MS-002's TC-105 supplies the collapsed half).
     print(
         f"[TC-108 documented half] N={N_TURNS} format=documented "
         f"write_ops={report['total_ops']} write_bytes={report['total_bytes']} "
         f"files={len(listing(sid_dir))} expected_ops=2N={2 * N_TURNS} "
         f"expected_files=N+1={N_TURNS + 1}"
     )

     @pytest.mark.parametrize("kwargs", [
         pytest.param({"json": {"system": [None]}}, id="system-null-block"),
         pytest.param({"json": {"messages": [{"content": [None]}]}}, id="content-null-block"),
         pytest.param({"json": {}}, id="empty-object"),
         pytest.param({"data": b"{", "headers": {"Content-Type": "application/json"}},
                      id="unparseable-body"),
     ])
     async def test_a_shape_base_already_tolerated_stays_tolerated(self, proxy, kwargs):
         """Green in both states, deliberately: this is the over-reach guard.
 
         `{"system":[null]}` and `{"messages":[{"content":[null]}]}` survive
         `_normalize_system`'s and `_strip_cache_control_from_content`'s own isinstance
         guards, and `{}`/an unparseable body reach the route as an empty dict. All four
-        answer 200 at base, at `6508941` and at HEAD (evidence §1, §2), so a pass that
-        narrows this route's normalization too far — or that hardens the loops and
-        changes what the estimate is computed from — reddens here.
+        answer 200 at base, at `6508941` and at HEAD (evidence §1, §2).
+
+        What reddens each direction, measured three ways on `6a2069b`: a key narrowed
+        past the billing nonce reddens `TestCountTokensKeysOnTheNormalizedDigest`;
+        hardened element loops redden `TestMessagesMalformedMemberPosture`; routing the
+        estimate through `normalize(body, session_id="")` reddens
+        `test_a_malformed_member_still_answers_an_estimate`. This class's own
+        over-reach direction is a stricter `_read_body` that rejects the unparseable
+        body with 400 — not a narrower key or a hardened loop.
         """
         response = await proxy.post(_COUNT, **kwargs)
 
         assert response.status == 200, "the repair reached a body base already tolerated"
         assert "input_tokens" in await response.json()
 
     async def test_a_non_mapping_body_still_answers_500(self, proxy):
         """Characterization, not a goal: base 500s here too, and so must this route.
 
         Base raises out of `_session_id` (`buster_ripper.py:449`), pre-range out of
         `session.py:95`, HEAD out of `normalize.py:52` — same status, same request, the
         raise site moved but nothing else (evidence §1, §2). This witness is the ceiling
         on the repair's tolerance: it reddens the moment anyone makes the route answer
         200 to a body base refused.
         """
         response = await proxy.post(_COUNT, json=[1, 2])
 
         assert response.status == 500, "the route now accepts a body base rejected"
 
 
 class TestMessagesMalformedMemberPosture:
     """`/v1/messages` still answers base's 500 — the drift guard for §RM-012.
 
     The rejected candidate for `CR4-F-01` was to harden `normalize()`'s element loops
     instead of narrowing this one call. That would fix the count_tokens column by moving
     `/v1/messages` from base's generated 500 to a proxy-authored 502 (evidence §7) — the
     route `G-003` exists to hold still, with an unauthorized ruling on what a proxy owes
     schema-invalid input doing the work. Any witness in this class reddens exactly then.
     """
 
     @pytest.mark.parametrize("body", _MALFORMED_MEMBERS)
     async def test_a_malformed_member_still_answers_bas_own_500(self, proxy, mocker, body):
         """Red first in the direction of the candidate, not of the shipped code.
 
         Green today because the raise happens in `normalize()` before the relay is
         reached. Hardening the loops lets the request through, so the upstream call is
         made and the answer becomes a 502 — base's authority for this route is
         `buster_ripper.py:374`/`:402`, which raise the identical `AttributeError`, and
         `roadmap.md` §RM-012 owns any change to that posture.
 
         The upstream call is stubbed to refuse rather than pointed at a socket, so the
         502 arm this witness describes is the same one `T013`'s review accepted.
         """
         mocker.patch.dict(session_mod._stats, {}, clear=True)
         sent = mocker.patch.object(proxy.server.app["session"], "post",
                                    side_effect=aiohttp.ClientConnectionError("stub refused"))
 
         response = await proxy.post(_MESSAGES, json=body)
 
         assert response.status == 500, (
             "/v1/messages stopped answering base's 500 — see roadmap.md §RM-012")
         assert not sent.called, "the request survived normalization and reached the relay"
 
     async def test_a_well_formed_body_still_reaches_the_relay(self, proxy, mocker):
         """The pair that keeps the witness above from passing on a dead route.
 
         Same stub, opposite expectation: a body normalization accepts must reach the
         upstream call and relay its answer, so a 500 in the class above is attributable
         to `normalize()` rather than to a route that never forwards anything.
         """
         sent = mocker.patch.object(
             proxy.server.app["session"], "post",
             return_value=StubUpstream(body=_ANSWER, headers=_JSON))
 
         response = await proxy.post(_MESSAGES, json={
             "model": "m", "max_tokens": 10,
             "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "hi"}],
         })
 
         assert response.status == 200
         assert sent.called, "the control body never reached the relay"

     @pytest.mark.parametrize("fmt", COLLAPSED_FORMATS)
     def test_sessions_buffer_independently(self, dump_env, fmt):
         """Two system prompts produce two states, each with its own records."""
         config.DUMP_FORMAT = fmt
         bodies_a = _turn_bodies("sys-a", 2)
         bodies_b = _turn_bodies("sys-b", 3)
         for body in bodies_a + bodies_b:
             dump_turn(body)
 
         state_a = dump._sessions[session_id(bodies_a[0])]
         state_b = dump._sessions[session_id(bodies_b[0])]
         assert state_a is not state_b
         assert len(state_a.pending) == 2
         assert len(state_b.pending) == 3
 
     @pytest.mark.parametrize("fmt", COLLAPSED_FORMATS)
     @pytest.mark.parametrize("text", ["é", "😀", "hello"])
     def test_utf8_pending_bytes_are_session_local(self, dump_env, fmt, text):
         """Each session counts rendered UTF-8 bytes, not records or characters."""
         config.DUMP_FORMAT = fmt
         a = _body("sys-a", [text])
         b = _body("sys-b", ["other"])
         dump_turn(a)
         dump_turn(b)
 
         state_a = dump._sessions[session_id(a)]
         state_b = dump._sessions[session_id(b)]
         assert state_a.pending_bytes == len(_render_body(a).encode("utf-8"))
         assert state_b.pending_bytes == len(_render_body(b).encode("utf-8"))
         dump.flush_all()
         assert state_a.pending_bytes == state_b.pending_bytes == 0
         assert len(state_a.pending) == len(state_b.pending) == 0
 
     @pytest.mark.parametrize("fmt", COLLAPSED_FORMATS)
     def test_state_advances_at_append_time(self, dump_env, fmt):
         """turn/prev_text advance on every call, together with the buffer."""
         config.DUMP_FORMAT = fmt
         bodies = _turn_bodies("sys-a", 3)
         sid = session_id(bodies[0])
         for i, body in enumerate(bodies):
             dump_turn(body)
             state = dump._sessions[sid]
             assert state.turn == i + 1
             assert state.prev_text == _render_body(body)
             assert len(state.pending) == i + 1
 
 
 class TestDocumentedRunWitness:
     """claim-93's documented half, as one fixed-format run.
 
     The format is never changed between turns: documented turns write their
     files immediately, the pending list stays empty, and no collapsed .log
     artifact is ever created.
     """
 
     def test_immediate_files_empty_pending_no_log(self, dump_env):
         """Each turn lands on disk at once; nothing buffers; no .log exists."""
         bodies = _turn_bodies("sys-a", 3)
         sid = session_id(bodies[0])
         for i, body in enumerate(bodies):
             dump_turn(body)
             state = dump._sessions[sid]
             expected = f"turn_{i:03d}.json" if i == 0 else f"turn_{i:03d}.diff"
             assert (dump_env / sid / expected).is_file()
             assert state.pending == []
             assert state.pending_bytes == 0
 
         assert len((dump_env / sid / "index.txt").read_text().splitlines()) == 3
         assert list((dump_env / sid).glob("*.log")) == []

 def test_billing_nonce_stripped_from_system_list():
     body = {

             {"type": "text", "text": "x-anthropic-billing-header: cc_version=2.1.50; cch=abc123;\nReal system prompt."},

 def test_billing_nonce_only_block_removed():
     """A system block containing only the billing nonce is dropped entirely."""
     body = {
         "system": [
             {"type": "text", "text": "x-anthropic-billing-header: cc_version=2.1.50; cch=abc123;\n"},
             {"type": "text", "text": "Keep this."},
         ],
         "messages": [],
     }
     out, changes = normalize(body)
     assert len(out["system"]) == 1
     assert out["system"][0]["text"] == "Keep this."
     assert any("billing" in c for c in changes)

         "system": "x-anthropic-billing-header: cch=xyz;\nSystem prompt here.",
         "messages": [],
     }
     out, changes = normalize(body)
     assert out["system"] == "System prompt here."
     assert any("billing" in c for c in changes)

     out, changes = normalize(body)
     assert out["system"][0]["text"] == "Clean system prompt."

 # ── SessionStats.update ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 
 
 def test_first_update_sets_latency_directly():
     s = SessionStats()
     s.update(input_tokens=1000, latency_ms=5000.0)
     assert s.input_tokens == 1000
     assert s.avg_latency_ms == 5000.0
     assert s.request_count == 1
 
 
 def test_second_update_applies_ema():
     s = SessionStats()
     s.update(1000, 5000.0)
     s.update(2000, 10000.0, ema_alpha=0.3)
     assert s.input_tokens == 2000
     assert s.avg_latency_ms == pytest.approx(6500.0)
     assert s.request_count == 2
 
 
 def test_ema_smooths_spike():
     s = SessionStats()
     for _ in range(5):
         s.update(50_000, 3000.0)
     assert s.avg_latency_ms < 5000
 
     s.update(50_000, 30_000.0)
     assert s.avg_latency_ms < 15_000
 
 
 def test_input_tokens_always_latest():
     s = SessionStats()
     s.update(100_000, 1000.0)
     s.update(120_000, 2000.0)
     s.update(50_000, 1000.0)
     assert s.input_tokens == 50_000

 # ── find_message_start ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 
 
 def _make_sse_chunk(event: dict) -> bytes:
     return f"data: {json.dumps(event)}\n\n".encode()
 
 
 def test_find_message_start_basic():
     evt = {
         "type": "message_start",
         "message": {"id": "msg_abc", "usage": {"input_tokens": 9876, "output_tokens": 0}},
     }
     result = find_message_start(_make_sse_chunk(evt))
     assert result is not None
     assert result["type"] == "message_start"
     assert result["message"]["usage"]["input_tokens"] == 9876

             return app

 def _arm_shared_session(mocker, raw_proxy, deadline: float) -> aiohttp.ClientSession:
     """Arm the app's own shared session to `deadline`, as base armed it to 600.
 
     The number comes from the construction this task must leave alone (``server.py:
     66-68``) and ``ClientSession`` has no setter for it, so the witness changes the
     number on the object already wired — one connector, one posture, still the one the
     app built. A short deadline is what lets a test run watch a stream straddle a
     timeout that would otherwise need ten minutes of upstream to reach.
     """
     session = raw_proxy.app["session"]
     mocker.patch.object(session, "_timeout", aiohttp.ClientTimeout(total=deadline))
     return session
 
 
 def _status_lines(raw: bytes) -> list:
     return [line for line in raw.split(b"\r\n") if line.startswith(b"HTTP/")]
 
 
 class TestNoRequestBodyCap:
     """CR3-F-01: no route answers 413 for body size, at any size the base stack accepted."""
 
     @pytest.mark.parametrize("case", [
         pytest.param(("/v1/messages", {"json": _MESSAGES}, "post"), id="messages"),
         pytest.param(("/v1/chat/completions", {"json": _CHAT}, "post"), id="chat-completions"),
         pytest.param(("/v1/models", {"data": _PASSTHROUGH}, "request"),
                      id="passthrough-catch-all"),
     ])
     async def test_a_two_mebibyte_body_reaches_the_upstream(self, proxy, mocker, case):
         """Red at pre-fix bytes as a 413 out of aiohttp's reader, before any handler
         ran: the cap is the application's, so it is one construction site to change."""
         path, kwargs, method = case
         sent = mocker.patch.object(
             proxy.server.app["session"], method,
             return_value=StubUpstream(body=_ANSWER, headers=_JSON))
 
         response = await proxy.request("POST", path, **kwargs)
 
         assert response.status == 200, "the app answered for the body size"
         assert sent.called, "the read never happened, so nothing was forwarded"
 
     async def test_count_tokens_reads_a_two_mebibyte_body_it_never_forwards(self, proxy):
         """The fourth route class answers from its own body, so its read path shows up as
         a token estimate rather than as an upstream call."""
         response = await proxy.post("/v1/messages/count_tokens",
                                     json=dict(_CHAT, max_tokens=10))
 
         assert response.status == 200, "the app answered for the body size"
         assert "input_tokens" in await response.json()
 
     async def test_a_strip_images_body_over_the_cap_reaches_the_image_handler(
         self, proxy, mocker
     ):
         """The cap sat *ahead* of the feature that shrinks this very body: with
         ``--strip-images`` on, an image-bearing request over 1 MiB was refused before one

         mocker.patch.object(proxy.server.app["session"], "post",
                             return_value=StubUpstream(body=_ANSWER, headers=_JSON))

             "content": [{"type": "image", "source": {
                 "type": "base64", "media_type": "image/png", "data": _HUGE}}],
         }])
 
         response = await proxy.post("/v1/messages", json=body)
 
         assert response.status == 200, "the cap still answers before the handler reads"
         assert stripped.called, "the image never reached the writer that strips it"

         assert json.loads(await response.read()) == {"error": "Invalid JSON"}

 class TestUndecodableUpstreamAnswer:
     """CR3-F-04(a)/(b): a payload the response cannot decode is still the upstream's answer."""
 
     async def test_the_non_stream_probe_decodes_the_body_it_is_only_checking(self, proxy,
                                                                             mocker):

     async def test_a_stream_error_body_that_no_charset_can_decode_still_answers_itself(
         self, proxy, mocker
     ):

         — not a ``ClientError`` either, so ``_stream_single_request``'s single arm matched
         nothing and the 502 this site declares was never produced. The fallback keeps the
         shape the arm has always had: the upstream's status, and its payload with the
         undecodable runs replaced."""
         mocker.patch.object(proxy.server.app["session"], "post",

                                                       body=_UNDECODABLE))
 
         response = await proxy.post("/v1/messages", json={**_MESSAGES, "stream": True})
 
         assert response.status == 503, "the proxy answered the upstream's payload with a 500"
         assert await response.text() == _UNDECODABLE.decode(errors="replace")

--- qa skill ---
---
name: qa-project
description: Project QA harness contract for buster-ripper — how to start the proxy, drive it, deliver signals, and capture evidence for QA gates. Read before authoring or executing any QA gate test in this project.
---

# QA Project Contract — buster-ripper

Project-specific QA harness declaration. The generic scenario-contract methodology
lives in `qa-manual-testing`; THIS file declares what this project's harness
actually supports. QA plans, author passes (`QAP-*`), and gate tests must operate
within these declarations.

## Harness Declarations

| Item | Declaration |
|---|---|
| Start command | `.venv/bin/buster-ripper --host 127.0.0.1 --port <port> --upstream http://hyperipper.local:30000 --dump-dir <tmpdir>`. Add `--dump-format <encoding>` for a per-encoding gate, and `--dump-flush-interval <seconds>` / `--dump-flush-bytes <bytes>` to make the flush triggers deterministic. Endpoint corrected 2026-10-17 from `:30001`, which is a second Buster Ripper instance rather than the model server (`decisions.md` §US-009). |
| Base URL | `POST http://127.0.0.1:<port>/v1/messages` — the interception path that reaches the dump. The proxy listens locally; the upstream is remote. |
| Upstream | `http://hyperipper.local:30000` — the live remote vLLM-compatible endpoint. It serves the Anthropic Messages API (`/v1/messages`) natively, as well as `/v1/chat/completions`, so the proxy passes Anthropic requests through. Gates send `model: sloppus`, the shared alias for whatever model the server runs; any other name gets a 404 before generation. There is no local stub: **a gate run fails if that host is unreachable**. Endpoint corrected 2026-10-17 from `:30001`, which is a peer Buster Ripper proxy, not the model server (`decisions.md` §US-009). **Stated amendment — the upstream-TLS gate only (`MS-004`):** that one gate does not use this upstream. It starts its own loopback self-signed HTTPS peer (`https://127.0.0.1:<peer-port>`, certificate material committed with the gate) because neither posture of the upstream-TLS opt-out can be observed against a plain-HTTP host. The amendment is scoped to that gate: every other gate keeps the remote upstream above, the hermetic default suite is unchanged, and no other gate gains a local peer. |
| Test framework | pytest + pytest-asyncio + pytest-aiohttp + pytest-mock (the `[dependency-groups] dev` set in `pyproject.toml`); `asyncio_mode = "auto"` is set in `[tool.pytest.ini_options]`. |
| Test file location | `tests/qa/` — a separate tree from the mocked `tests/` suite. |
| Teardown | Send SIGTERM (or SIGINT) to the proxy subprocess and wait for exit, then inspect the dump directory. The signal path is the feature under test, so teardown is also a trigger. |
| Deterministic triggers | The harness fixes **both** flush parameters at process start — the interval and the volume threshold — through their CLI flags. Without them the timer gate and the volume gate are sleep-and-hope races, not tests. |
| Dump root | The `--dump-dir` value; a fresh temporary directory per gate. The proxy creates it at startup. |
| Session directory | `DUMP_DIR/<sid>/`, where `<sid>` is the first 12 hex characters of `sha256(system prompt)` (`session.py:session_id`). A gate that must know the directory either computes the id from the system prompt it sent or reads the single subdirectory the run created. |
| Artifact | `DUMP_DIR/<sid>/<encoding>.log` — one append-only artifact per `(session, encoding)`, where `<encoding>` is the configured `--dump-format` value. |
| Prerequisites | `uv sync` has run (`.venv/bin/buster-ripper` exists); the remote upstream is reachable. |

## Capability Declarations (for the planning-time QA Capability Gate)

- **Synchronization**: required. The proxy is a long-running server. **Readiness** —
  poll until the listening port accepts a connection (or until the startup log line
  appears) before sending a request. **Quiescence** — after SIGTERM/SIGINT, wait for
  process exit before inspecting `DUMP_DIR`; for the timer gate, poll the artifact
  (contents or mtime) rather than sleeping a fixed multiple of the interval.
- **Determinism**: required and available. Both flush triggers are injectable at
  process start (`--dump-flush-interval`, `--dump-flush-bytes`) and each gate uses a
  fresh temporary `--dump-dir`, so the flush points are chosen by the test rather
  than observed. **Caveat**: the upstream is a live remote service — request
  outcomes (model output, latency) are not under the harness's control. Gates must
  assert on the proxy's dump artifact and process behavior, never on model output.
- **Harness tier**: process-level end-to-end. The gates start the real proxy binary
  as a subprocess, send real HTTP requests, deliver real signals, and inspect the
  files it wrote. No in-process or mocked tier can prove the shutdown flush, the
  timer flush, or the volume flush — those depend on process lifecycle and on real
  signal delivery.
- **Evidence**: the dump artifact and the session directory listing; the process exit
  code; captured stdout/stderr (the proxy logs to stderr); and, for write-count
  claims, an instrumented write-bearing file operation on a dump artifact
  (`write_text`, or `open(..., "a"/"w")` + `write`; directory creation is not a
  write-bearing operation).

## Conventions

- **Hermetic default suite.** The gates under `tests/qa/` need the remote upstream,
  real subprocesses, and real signals. They are selected explicitly (by path or by
  marker) and are not part of the hermetic default `uv run pytest` run, which must
  keep passing without network access. The default run's coverage gate
  (`--cov=buster_ripper --cov-fail-under=80`, `[tool.pytest.ini_options] addopts`)
  governs the unit/integration suite, not the QA gates.
- **One session per gate.** Give each gate its own `--dump-dir` and its own system
  prompt so its session directory is unambiguous.
- **Signals are triggers, not teardown only.** A shutdown gate sends the signal and
  then asserts on both the artifact contents and the exit status.
- **The dump is opt-in.** Without `--dump-dir` the proxy never writes an artifact;
  a gate that expects one must pass the flag.

`--port`, `--dump-dir` exist today (`buster_ripper/main.py`), and

 def forward_headers(headers: dict) -> dict:
     """Build headers to send upstream, dropping hop-by-hop and host."""
     return {
         k: v
         for k, v in headers.items()
         if k.lower() not in ("host", "content-length", "connection", "transfer-encoding", *_HOP_BY_HOP)
     }
 
 
-def response_headers(upstream_headers: dict | CIMultiDictProxy) -> CIMultiDict:
+def response_headers(upstream_headers: Mapping[str, str]) -> CIMultiDict:
     """Build headers to send downstream, dropping hop-by-hop and content-length.
 
     One pair per *occurrence*: the client hands the upstream's head over as a
     per-occurrence mapping and ``web.Response`` writes one field-line per entry it is
     given, so a name the upstream sent twice (`Set-Cookie: a=1` then `b=2`, two `Vary`
     values) reaches the client twice. Folding into a plain ``dict`` here kept the last
     occurrence and destroyed the rest — and that was not base parity either, because
     base's client merged occurrences into one comma-joined value, so no value was ever
     lost on the way through.
 
     content-length is dropped alongside the hop-by-hop names because aiohttp
     re-derives it from the body we actually send: an upstream that answers with

 def index_lines(sid_dir: str) -> list[str]:
     text = read_text(os.path.join(sid_dir, "index.txt"))
     return [ln for ln in text.splitlines() if ln.strip()]
 
 
 def production_diff_text(previous: str, current: str) -> str:
     """The documented writer's published diff text for two public renderings.
 
-    ``README.md:154`` publishes ``turn_NNN.diff`` as a unified diff. The writer
+    ``README.md:165`` publishes ``turn_NNN.diff`` as a unified diff. The writer
     splits both renderings with ``splitlines(keepends=True)``, asks
     ``difflib.unified_diff`` for ``lineterm=""``, and joins the yielded lines
     with ``""`` — so each control line runs into the line that follows it. The gate
     mirrors that public shape here from the renderings alone and reads both sides
     with one reader (:func:`diff_changed_lines`); it never calls the
     implementation's own renderer or diff helper.
     """
     return "".join(difflib.unified_diff(
         previous.splitlines(keepends=True),
         current.splitlines(keepends=True),
         fromfile="previous", tofile="current", lineterm="",
     ))

-    """The ``-``/``+`` content lines of ``turn NNN-1 -> turn NNN``, per README.md:154."""
+    """The ``-``/``+`` content lines of ``turn NNN-1 -> turn NNN``, per README.md:165."""
     return diff_changed_lines(production_diff_text(previous, current))

codex
The functional test suite passes, but the claimed new lint gate cannot run from the project’s declared development environment and is not enforced.

Review comment:

- [P2] Make the new Ruff gate executable in the dev environment — /Users/alexhubitski/Projects/buster-ripper/pyproject.toml:25-28
  `ruff` is not included in the `dev` dependency group (and `.venv/bin/ruff` is absent), while this config is described as a gate. Consequently, a fresh `uv sync --group dev` cannot run the intended check, and no repository automation invokes it, so the new F-rule policy is unenforced. Add Ruff to the managed dev toolchain and invoke it from the project’s verification entrypoint.

- [P2] Make the new Ruff gate executable in the dev environment — /Users/alexhubitski/Projects/buster-ripper/pyproject.toml:25-28
  `ruff` is not included in the `dev` dependency group (and `.venv/bin/ruff` is absent), while this config is described as a gate. Consequently, a fresh `uv sync --group dev` cannot run the intended check, and no repository automation invokes it, so the new F-rule policy is unenforced. Add Ruff to the managed dev toolchain and invoke it from the project’s verification entrypoint.
